# RBAC Wave 1 — catalogue as code Implementation Plan (rev 4)

**Revision: rev 4** (2026-09-10) — fix round on **Codex plan gate r3**. Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-w1-codex-gate-r3.md` (CHANGES-REQUIRED, **6 BLOCKER / 2 MAJOR / 4 minor** plus the citation audit); prompt: `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-w1-round-3-2026-09-10.md`. The ID-by-ID mapping is the **Change log rev 3 → rev 4** section at the end of this document. *(Rev 3 was the fix round on gate r2 — register `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-w1-codex-gate-r2.md`, prompt `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-w1-round-2-2026-09-10.md`; rev 2 was the fix round on gate r1 — register `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-0b1-codex-gate-r1.md`.)*

> **Headline of rev 4, so a reader does not have to reconstruct it.** Six blockers, all in the machinery around the settled contracts rather than in the contracts themselves: **`permissions:sync-fleet`'s closure now takes the hydrated `Tenant`** 0b's runner hands it, instead of a `string` that would have TypeErrored on every tenant and been swallowed into `exception:` outcomes; **the planner canonicalises every virtual grant set**, so a legacy tenant whose template holds a rename source no longer previews green and then rolls back with `plan_divergence`; **`permissions:sync --tenant=B` restores tenant A** instead of leaving the process in no tenant context at all; **both PHPStan rules normalise site paths against an injected `projectRoot`**, so the generated shrink-only baselines actually absorb the pre-existing sites; **the after-commit cache flush sets its own team inside the callback**, which is the only thing that works under the outer transaction the compatibility registration path already opens; and **compatibility mode gains `permissions:sync --compat`**, one explicit, executable, non-production path where rev 3 documented behaviour no command could reach. Plus the two majors: the impossible two-mode missing-admin oracle is **split** into a green dry-run preview and a failing apply, and the literal guard's exemption becomes a **path prefix** instead of a `Permission.php` filename suffix that exempted unrelated production files. The ID-by-ID mapping is the **Change log rev 2 → rev 3** section at the end of this document. The headline changes: **`PermissionSyncService` PLANS over a virtual state before it writes, and every non-green apply rolls back**; **`permissions:sync --tenant=` initializes the tenant database**; **provisioning throws on a non-green sync**; **`permission-registry-keys.json` lands in `Phase 1.16`'s own commit**; **ten class/test bodies are supplied in full**; and **the catalogue is 327, not 325**, because wave 0b rev 3 made `lane/t2-receipt-spine` a whole-wave entry condition.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Replace the 871-line seeder array with a code-owned catalogue — per-module `PermissionManifest`s aggregated by a `PermissionRegistry` — and make a deploy apply an **additive template delta** that provably never touches a custom role or a customised system role. This is the wave that closes G3, the core gap: today the application never re-syncs an existing tenant, while staging runs a **destructive full reseed on every deploy** (I-20).

**Architecture:** A permission becomes a `PermissionDefinition` whose key is **derived** from `(resource, verb, qualifier)` and can therefore never disagree with its parts; verbs come from a **closed** `PermissionVerb` enum; each module owns a manifest in its `Domain/Authorization/` (a pure declaration with zero infrastructure dependency) and contributes it by **tagging** in its own service provider, which is the automatic-inclusion guarantee at the wiring level. `PermissionSyncService::sync()` runs nine steps in one transaction under the **wave-0b** `PermissionWriteLock`, with **adoption before any grant write**, and it never references a single symbol from `lane/w-lot-a-1a`. The seeder survives as a thin adapter, because two contracts (the exporter and that lane's staging protocol) name it.

**Tech Stack:** Laravel 12 / PHP 8.4 / Spatie laravel-permission 6.25 / Stancl Tenancy 3.10 / PHPUnit 11 / PHPStan L8 / Pint / nikic/php-parser / React 19 / Vitest

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` (**rev 9.1** — rev 9 ACCEPTED by Codex spec gate r9, plus editorial amendment **A-1** which moves item 0a-4 to wave 0b as **0b-15** and re-pins the ceilings at `152 / 142` with a **294**-key route baseline at the end of 0b). Wave 1's authoritative rows are §§4.2, 4.3.0a, 4.3.1–4.3.5, 4.5, 4.6.5, 4.7, 4.9, 5.2, 6 and 8's wave-1 row.

**Master plan:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-programme-execution-plan.md`.

**Sibling plans:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-0a.md` (rev 3), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-0b.md` (**rev 4**, committed `2f172dd5e` — the gate-r3 fix round; rev 3 was `692cede7a`). **Wave 1 inherits SIX artefacts from 0b and introduces none of them:** `PermissionRenameMap`, `LegacyRoleBaseline` (both predicates), `PermissionWriteLock`, `TenantFleetRunner` (+ `FleetRunResult` / `FleetTenantOutcome` / `FleetOutcome`), **`Tests\Architecture\Support\PermissionWriterCensus`** and **`TenantFleetRunnerSelectorTest::fleetCommands()`** — the last two are 0b additions this plan re-pins to by name (0b rev 4, *"Signature changes for wave 1"*, item 2 and the `fleetCommands()` row). It **deletes eight** wave-0b stopgap artefacts, all in one commit (Task 1.10).

> **The producer is wave 0b REV 4 (`2f172dd5e`), and FIVE of its shapes reach wave 1** (plan gate r2 **B2-6**, plan gate r3 citation audit; 0b rev 4's *"Signature changes for wave 1"*, which rev 4 corrects to say there are **no production signature changes at all** — the one contract change is a private test-fixture constant):
> 1. **`PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` is `array<string, list<string>>`** — a helper declares **every** entry point, not one. Wave 1's row is `'…/TemplateDeltaApplier.php::applyTo' => ['…/PermissionSyncService.php::sync', '…/RoleController.php::reapplyTemplate']`, and **both** of those go in `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`. 0b rules this **branch 2** — `applyTo()` acquires nothing and its callers are proven complete by cross-file enumeration — which is the branch this plan already assumed.
> 2. **`PermissionWriterCensus` carries three things wave 1 uses directly:** `collect()`, **`methodSource(array $unit)`** (AST-bounded, so a failure message quotes the right method) and **`callersOf(array $units, string $name)`** (the tree-wide caller enumeration the `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` row depends on). Wave 1 **consumes** them; it does not re-implement them.
> 3. **`lane/t2-receipt-spine` merged into local `dev` is a WHOLE-WAVE entry condition of 0b**, so by the time wave 1 opens T2's two transfer keys are in the tree. **The catalogue is therefore 327, not 325** — see the Global Constraint and **Q-w1-2**.
> 4. **The census's role-lock classifier changed shape in 0b rev 4, and Phase 0 verifies the NEW constants.** `ROLE_QUERY_ROOTS = ['Role']` (a last-name match) is **gone**, replaced by three constants: **`ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES`** (fully qualified, resolved through the file's `use` map — `2f172dd5e:…-wave-0b.md:3073-3077`), **`ROLE_TABLE`** (`'roles'`, so `DB::table('roles')` and `$this->db->table('roles')` count — `:3086`) and **`ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS`** (`['roles', 'Role']`, the fail-closed trigger for an unresolvable root — `:3099`). **`PermissionWriterVisitor::__construct` now takes two parameters**, `(string $relativePath, string $source)` (`:3251-3254`), because the lexical check reads the enclosing method's own bytes. Wave 1 adds three writer methods to that census and **must not** re-introduce the rev-3 constant name; a tree still carrying `ROLE_QUERY_ROOTS` means 0b shipped rev 3 and its scanner is red on twenty-three false offenders. Phase 0 greps for all four.
> 5. **`TenantFleetRunner`'s per-tenant callback is `Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome`** — the runner hydrates, checks readiness, initializes tenancy and calls the closure with the **model** (`:1486`, `:1570-1571`, `:1593`). `permissions:sync-fleet`'s closure therefore takes `Tenant $tenant` and derives `(string) $tenant->id` itself. Rev 3 of this plan wrote `string $tenantId`, which every tenant would have failed on (plan gate r3, **B3-1**).

## Entry condition

**Four things, and the fourth is not a repo fact.**

1. **Wave 0a merged into local `dev`.** Its ratchet and baseline are files this wave edits.
2. **Wave 0b merged into local `dev`.** Wave 1 deletes 0b's stopgap commands and consumes its six inherited artefacts.
3. **`lane/t2-receipt-spine` merged into local `dev`** (plan gate r2 **B2-6**). This is **inherited**, not new: 0b rev 3 makes T2 a whole-wave entry condition of 0b itself, so a merged 0b implies a merged T2. Wave 1 nonetheless asserts it **directly**, because wave 1's catalogue arithmetic — **327** — depends on T2's `inventory.transfers.reconcile` and `inventory.transfers.close` being in the tree (`lane/t2-receipt-spine:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:201-202`, granted to `manager` at `:604`). **If T2 is not an ancestor, the catalogue is 325 and every count, manifest, enum and fixture in this plan is wrong by two — STOP and reconcile with the orchestrator rather than editing the numbers down.**
4. **`lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s five-push staging protocol COMPLETE — not merely its merge, and additionally wave 0b DEPLOYED to staging with `permissions:ensure-fleet` green.**

   The staging-protocol half is spec §8's own condition: that protocol asserts pre/post role-permission snapshots on staging boots, and a registry-driven sync running between the snapshots invalidates the comparison. **No grep can prove it** — it is a staging fact. The orchestrator confirms it from the lane's evidence.

   The 0b-deployed half is this plan's addition, and it is mechanical rather than a matter of taste: `LegacyRoleBaseline::version0()` is `postA1aGrants ∪ wave0bAdditions`, and adoption compares a tenant against it. A tenant that received the code of 0b but **not its `permissions:ensure-fleet` deploy** holds `postA1aGrants` alone, compares **unequal** to v0, and is adopted **customised** — permanently withholding the D2 deltas it is entitled to, for a reason that is a deploy omission rather than an operator choice. The check:
   ```
   # on staging, per tenant, before wave 1 deploys
   cat /var/run/autoerp/permissions-ensure.status     # expect: permissions_ensure=ok
   grep 'PERMISSIONS-ENSURE-FLEET' <the 0b deploy log>  # expect: failed=0 blocked=0
   ```
   > **0b rev 2 moved the writer of that file.** The status file is written by **`scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`**, the two-invocation wrapper, not by `permissions:ensure-fleet` itself (0b rev 2, BLOCKER B0b-6). The check above is unchanged; what changed is who wrote the line. Task 1.10 deletes that script with the commands it wraps and Task 1.11 replaces the file with `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status`.

   Any tenant listed in a 0b run's `admin_only=` field is a **known** customised role and adopts customised **correctly** — that is residual R-0b-7 and it is the intended outcome, not this condition's concern.

**Mechanical checks for conditions 1, 2 and 3:**
```
cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp
git fetch --all --prune
git merge-base --is-ancestor lane/rbac-w0a dev        && echo "0a MERGED" || echo "0a NOT MERGED — STOP"
git merge-base --is-ancestor lane/rbac-w0b dev        && echo "0b MERGED" || echo "0b NOT MERGED — STOP"
git merge-base --is-ancestor lane/t2-receipt-spine dev && echo "T2 MERGED" || echo "T2 NOT MERGED — STOP"
git log dev --oneline -1 -- apps/api/app/Console/Commands/EnsurePermissionsFleet.php   # must return a commit
git log dev --oneline -1 -- scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh                           # must return a commit
git log dev --oneline -1 -- apps/api/tests/Architecture/Support/PermissionWriterCensus.php  # must return a commit
grep -c "inventory.transfers.reconcile" apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php   # must be >= 2 (definition + manager grant)
```
The check is `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, **never** a `git branch --merged` grep: a grep matches a substring and matches a deleted-then-recreated branch name (0b rev 3's Global Constraints, same reason).

**Overlap.** Wave 1 **owns** `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php` end to end and cannot run concurrently with any lane that edits it. Before opening the lane, prove no in-flight lane claims it:
```
for lane in $(git branch --list 'lane/*' --format='%(refname:short)'); do
  git merge-base --is-ancestor "$lane" dev && continue
  echo "== $lane"; git diff --name-only "dev...$lane" -- apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php
done
```
**Expected: no output under any lane.** Any hit is a blocker, not a merge conflict to resolve later.

## Global Constraints

- [ ] **The catalogue is 327 keys.** `304` at `dev` (`dev:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:48-559`, `permissionNames()`) **+ 2** added by the W-LOT wrapper (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:91-94` — `batches.recall.request` and `treasury.manage_all_locations`, which are **not** inside the 304) **+ 19** declared by wave 0b **+ 2** added by `lane/t2-receipt-spine` (`inventory.transfers.reconcile` and `inventory.transfers.close`, defined at `lane/t2-receipt-spine:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:201-202` and granted to `manager` at `:604`) **= 327**. The eleven renames change spellings, not the count. **Every count, fixture, precondition and comment in this plan says 327**; rev 1's `304 + 19 = 323` was arithmetically wrong (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B1-1) and rev 2's **325** omitted T2, which wave 0b rev 3 made a whole-wave entry condition (plan gate r2, BLOCKER **B2-6**).
- [ ] **No commit in this wave is knowingly red.** Every task states the exact run list for its own commit, and a test whose oracle does not yet exist ships in the commit that creates the oracle. Task 1.4's nine module-group commits stay green through a **shrink-only `permission-catalogue-unassigned.json`** fixture (Task 1.3), not through a full-registry parity assertion that cannot hold until the last manifest.
- [ ] **Nothing in this wave references a symbol from `lane/w-lot-a-1a`.** Not `LotActionPermissionDelta`, not `LotActionPermissionActivation`, not any `TemplateMigration*` class (there is none — the whole machinery is deleted from the design). `PermissionSyncServiceTest::sync_references_no_lot_action_symbol` is the mechanical form. The delta runs **once, at that lane's own activation deploy, before wave 1 exists**; the registry's `template_version = 0` snapshots are the *post-A-1a* state, so sync starts from what tenants actually hold and has nothing to re-apply.
- [ ] **Adoption runs before any grant write.** Steps are rename → create → orphan report → **adopt** → create missing template roles → template deltas → deprecation replacements → admin → after-commit cache. Running a role-mutating step first is what let rev 3 of the spec re-add a grant a tenant had removed, making a customised role compare equal and be adopted pristine.
- [ ] **The lock is 0b's, unchanged.** `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` on the key `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId`; `FOR UPDATE` ordered `roles.name` ASC then `roles.id` ASC. Wave 1 **adds three writer methods** to 0b's AST census, and it must use 0b rev 3's **three-way** split with the **list-valued** `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`:
  - `PermissionSyncService::sync()` → **`LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`** (it calls `acquire()` as the first statement inside its transaction);
  - `RoleController::update` (the `customised_at` writer) and `RoleController::reapplyTemplate` → **`LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`**;
  - **`TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo()` → `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`**, whose value is a **`list<string>` naming BOTH entry points** — 0b rev 3 widened the constant from `array<string, string>` to `array<string, list<string>>` for exactly this row, and its ruling is **branch 2**: `applyTo()` acquires nothing, and `PermissionWriterCensus::callersOf()` proves the declared list is the **complete** set of callers **tree-wide**, not merely within the helper's own file. The literal wave 1 writes is:
    ```php
    'app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/TemplateDeltaApplier.php::applyTo' => [
        'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/PermissionSyncService.php::sync',
        'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::reapplyTemplate',
    ],
    ```
    **If wave 1 discovers a third caller, the fixture grows and the test names it** — it is not a reason to fall back to branch 1 (an `acquire()` inside the applier) without saying so (0b rev 3, *"Signature changes for wave 1"*; plan gate r1 MAJOR M1-3; plan gate r2 rev-1 closure row *"M1-3 — inherited lock proof"*).
  - **Wave 1 consumes `PermissionWriterCensus::methodSource(array $unit)` and `::callersOf(array $units, string $name)`; it does not re-implement either.** `methodSource()` is AST-bounded, so a failure message quotes the right method body rather than the whole file; `callersOf()` is the cross-file enumeration the `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` row's completeness proof rests on.

  Wave 1 does **not** redefine the key, the hash function or the row order, and the `INITIALIZATION_ONLY` fixture **must not grow**.
- [ ] **Every grant set the planner compares is CANONICAL.** `PermissionRenameMap::canonicalise()` is applied to each virtual role's grants as they enter the projection, to the template and admin targets before each diff, and to both sides of the executor's divergence check. The permission row is renamed **in place** at execution time, so the applier re-reads post-rename names; a planner comparing pre-rename names computes a delta the applier does not, and **every ordinary legacy tenant** rolls back with `plan_divergence` after a green dry run (plan gate r3, **B3-2**).
- [ ] **Wave 1 writes NO new fleet iterator, and its closure takes the model 0b's runner hands it.** `permissions:sync-fleet` passes `TenantFleetRunner::run()` a `Closure(Tenant): FleetTenantOutcome` — the type 0b declares (`2f172dd5e:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-0b.md:1486`) and calls with the hydrated model (`:1593`). A `string` parameter TypeErrors on every tenant and is swallowed into `exception:` outcomes (plan gate r3, **B3-1**).
- [ ] **A per-tenant command RESTORES the context it found; only a run that started from central ENDS one.** Stancl's `initialize()` already ends the active tenant (`vendor/stancl/tenancy/src/Tenancy.php:48-50`) and `end()` nulls it (`:73`), so `finally { end(); }` after `initialize(B)` destroys tenant A rather than restoring it (plan gate r3, **B3-3**).
- [ ] **Compatibility mode has exactly ONE executable command — `permissions:sync --compat`** — global, central-context, steps 1–4, no tenancy init, no fleet, no readiness predicate, no permissions team, marker token `tenant=compat`. It is the **non-production topology** and is deliberately absent from the deploy runbook; `permissions:sync-fleet` refuses in it with a typed message (plan gate r3, **B3-6**).
- [ ] **Two tenant migrations, timestamped AFTER `2026_09_06_205000_add_provisioning_source_to_roles.php`.** Wave 1 ships **one** of them (`roles`); `users` is wave 2. Neither may write `tenant_id`, or the W-LOT CHECK's `tenant_id IS NOT NULL` clause interacts with a NULL-team legacy role.
- [ ] **`provisioning_source` is written in EXACTLY ONE case** and never cleared, changed or widened: when step 5 **creates** a missing `general_manager` on a tenant that carries no marker. `PermissionSyncMarkerTest` asserts the five negatives and the one positive.
- [ ] **A custom role (`is_system = false`) receives no template grant, ever, and never loses a key of any kind** — the sole exception being a `replacedBy` grant on a deprecation, which emits a `RoleSyncedV1` audit event so the exception is visible in the audit chain rather than being an invisible carve-out.
- [ ] **A customised system role (`customised_at IS NOT NULL`) receives nothing in either direction.** Not a grant, not a revocation.
- [ ] **An UNCUSTOMISED template-linked role is brought to set EQUALITY with its template** — every key the template declares and the role lacks is granted, and **every key the role holds that the template does not declare is revoked, deprecated or not**. That is safe *here and only here*: by construction such a role is its template plus whatever sync itself put there.
- [ ] **`admin` is a full replace to `registry->activeKeys()`**, and `admin` is implicit — a manifest that lists `'admin'` in `templateDefaults` is a build error.
- [ ] **Idempotency has two clauses, both mechanically checked:** every **`mutations`** counter zero **AND** zero database writes. The two **`gauges`** (`orphans`, `templates_skipped_customised`) report whatever is true of the tenant and are **unconstrained** on a re-run. `outcome=ALREADY_CURRENT` is defined as exactly that conjunction.
- [ ] **`--dry-run` is WRITE-free, not FAILURE-free, and it is the SAME PLAN.** `PermissionSyncService` computes a **complete nine-step plan over a VIRTUAL state** — a `SyncPlan` built from in-memory projections of what steps 1–8 would do, including the adoption verdicts and the roles step 5 would create — **before any write happens**. Apply mode then **executes that plan**; dry-run **renders the same plan and executes nothing**. The two paths therefore produce **byte-identical marker lines** for the same tenant state, which is what makes step 2 of the deploy a real preview.

  Three consequences, each of them a fix for a defect rev 2 shipped (plan gate r2, BLOCKER **B2-1**):
  - a legacy tenant whose roles carry `template_key = NULL` is **adopted in the virtual state**, so the dry run computes its template deltas instead of skipping them;
  - a tenant missing `general_manager` (or `admin`) has that role **created in the virtual state**, so the dry run reports the full grant delta and does **not** falsely report `admin_role_missing`;
  - **any non-green outcome on the APPLYING path leaves the transaction by ROLLBACK, never by ordinary return.** `PermissionSyncService::sync()` throws **`SyncRollback`** — the wave-1 sentinel, renamed from `SyncDryRunRollback` because the dry run is no longer its only cause — whenever `outcome->isGreen()` is false, so a `FAILED`/`BLOCKED` apply writes nothing at all. `PermissionSyncServiceTest::a_missing_admin_role_fails_closed` and `::a_non_green_apply_rolls_the_whole_run_back` prove it by **row counts on `permissions`, `roles` and `role_has_permissions` before and after**, on both paths — not by "no exception was thrown".

  A dry run that reaches `BLOCKED` still exits **2**; one that fails still exits **1**. On the per-tenant command and on the fleet runner alike. Wave 1 ships `SyncRollback` in the same commit that deletes 0b's `DryRunRollback`; there is never a moment at which the dry-run path has no sentinel.
- [ ] **On a non-green outcome the counters describe the REJECTED PLAN, not rows written.** `outcome=FAILED` / `outcome=BLOCKED` on the marker line is what says nothing landed; the `mutations` numbers beside it say what *would* have landed had the run been green. That is deliberate — it is the same number the dry run prints, which is the whole point of one planner — and the reviewer gate asks for it by name.
- [ ] **Every wave from 1 onward deploys through `permissions:sync-fleet`** — never `tenants:run permissions:sync` (discarded child exit statuses), never the per-tenant `permissions:sync` without an explicit `--tenant=` in an operator-remediation context, and there is **no `--force`**.
- [ ] **The migration runs before every sync, dry run included.** `tenants:migrate-rolling` is step 1 of the deploy and `permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` is step 2 — the human Deploy table, the machine-readable YAML runbook and `PermissionDeploySequenceTest`'s exact-order assertion all say the same thing, because sync's adoption and delta logic reads and writes `template_key`, `template_version`, `customised_at` and `is_system`. Rev 1's Deploy table put the dry run first and its first dry run would have failed on missing columns (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B1-5).
- [ ] **Constructor injection only** (`private readonly`), never `app()` — with **one** sanctioned exception, named in Task 1.14: the two `public static` shims on `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`, which have no constructor to inject into and exist purely to keep the exporter's and the lane's contracts intact. They carry an explicit `@internal` docblock and are listed among the PHPStan rule's allowed call sites.
- [ ] **Strict types** in every new PHP file, no `mixed`, no `any`. **Enums** for every status/type value.
- [ ] **PHPUnit by path only.** Never the full suite locally.
- [ ] **PG lane env AND the repository's PG harness.** `apps/api/phpunit-pgsql.xml:2-16` documents the harness explicitly, so every PG command in this plan is:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api && \
        DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 \
        DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w1 DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w1_central \
        php artisan test -c phpunit-pgsql.xml <paths>
      ```
      Both database variables, always; never the shared default; never two PG legs in parallel. Rev 1 exported the variables and then ran the default PHPUnit configuration (plan gate r1, MAJOR M1-6).
- [ ] **Commit subjects are `Phase 1.<task>: <imperative summary>`** — `AGENTS.md:15-16`. Every task below states its **final** subject at its own commit site; rev 1's eighteen `feat(...)` subjects are withdrawn (plan gate r1, MAJOR M1-1).
- [ ] **Path-scoped commits with an exact `git add` list at every commit site**, both trailers on every commit:
      ```
      Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
      Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Bp1WuD39YqaMWiHR8vPDNN
      ```
      Where a task says *"commit (`<subject>`, both trailers)"*, it means: that subject line, a body explaining **why** (not what — the diff says what), and then **exactly these two lines**, in this order, as the last two lines of the message. Two tasks (1.10 and 1.14) spell the whole message out because their bodies carry rulings a reviewer must read; the rest do not, and the shorthand is not a licence to omit the trailers.
- [ ] **Worktree:** `git worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w1 -b lane/rbac-w1 dev`, from the shared checkout. Never edit or commit in the shared `dev` worktree.
- [ ] **No `origin` push.** Merge into **local** `dev`; the owner promotes.
- [ ] **Empirical evidence, not compilation.** Task 1.19 names the UI probes and the one-week staging soak. This wave changes what a deploy does to every tenant's roles — "tests pass" is not the evidence.

### Convention 09 (second-of-everything)

`roles` and `permissions` are **not** `CATALOGUE_TABLES` entities and this wave does not make them so (D1 keeps roles tenant-scoped by ruling). Both are already in `TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest`'s exclusion map with stated reasons (`apps/api/tests/Architecture/TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest.php:131-132,160`). **The obligation is not to add an exclusion but not to invalidate an existing one:** `roles_tenant_template_key_unique` **carries `tenant_id`** and would satisfy the ratchet's shape rule even if `roles` were ever swept, while `roles_global_template_key_unique` is deliberately tenant-less because it constrains exactly the `tenant_id IS NULL` legacy rows. **Wave 1 must confirm the ratchet stays green rather than editing it.**

The three behavioural axes are real tests (spec §6, wave 1 row):

| Axis | Test | Assertion, on **data meaning** |
|---|---|---|
| **Three tenant types** | `PermissionSyncAdoptionTest` | the three states of §4.3.0a: **legacy unmarked** (`manager` adopts customised — a real, non-normalisable difference; the other six pristine; step 5 then creates `general_manager` and writes the marker), **marked by the lane and untouched** (all eight adopt **pristine**), **provisioned after wave 1** (adoption is a no-op because the roles already carry a `template_key`). |
| **Second company** | `PermissionsSyncTest::sync_creates_every_registry_permission_in_a_fresh_tenant_and_in_a_second_tenant` | **two physical tenant databases**; tenant B's `admin` holds exactly `activeKeys()`; tenant B's customised `manager` is skipped **independently** of tenant A's. |
| **Second location** | `PosApprovalPermissionSecondLocationTest` | a second `pos_enabled` location; a POS approval permission granted by sync is honoured at the **selected** terminal's location, not the default. |
| **Re-run / idempotency** | `PermissionsSyncTest::a_second_run_writes_no_row` | `ALREADY_CURRENT`, every `mutations` counter **zero**, **zero write statements by query-log count**, and the two gauges asserted **unchanged, not zero**. Plus: sync on a tenant the lane's delta already ran against changes neither W-LOT key, changes no `general_manager` grant, and **writes no `provisioning_source`**. |

## Decisions and open questions

Six. Four are the spec's own **OQ-1..OQ-4**, of which **two must be answered before this wave's baselines are pinned**. Two are gaps this plan had to resolve; each names the bounded edit for the alternative. **Plan gate r1 assessed all three `Q-w1-*` and none of them is an owner question** — Q-w1-1 needs a docblock edit only, Q-w1-2 was an arithmetic defect (closed below), Q-w1-3 is binding lane compatibility.

### O-5 / OQ-3 — **owner-owed, and it blocks Task 1.13's baseline**

Which, if any, of the **eighteen** baselined SoD combinations should be retired? The applied default (spec §9.2): retire **three** from `manager` — `invoices.create` + `.post`, `credit-notes.create` + `.post`, `payments.create` + `.refund` — keep the rest, change **nothing** on `accountant`. Second half of the question: does `general_manager` **mirror** the retirement (18 → 15 → 12)?

**This plan ships the baseline unchanged at eighteen and the ceiling at eighteen**, per the spec's *"the design ships the baseline unchanged until it is made"*. **If the owner rules before Task 1.13**, the bounded edit is: remove the retired keys from those templates' `templateDefaults` in the manifests of Task 1.4c and 1.4g, regenerate `permission-sod-baseline.json`, and lower `SOD_BASELINE_CEILING` to 15 or 12. **If the owner rules after this wave merges**, it is a follow-up lane and the ceiling falls then — the ratchet guarantees the number can only fall, so nothing is lost by waiting. **Ask before pinning the baseline.**

### O-6 / OQ-4 — **owner-owed at the END of this wave, not at its start**

Does `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT` default to `true` in production after the soak? The applied default: **yes**, after a one-week staging soak with zero unintended diffs. The conservative alternative — leave it `false` and run `permissions:sync-fleet` as a named deploy step — is a real option and costs one runbook line. **This plan ships the flag's production default at `false` and the soak as Task 1.19's exit condition.** The flag name is **kept**: W-LOT-A-1a's evidence protocol asserts its value on staging in six places, and renaming it would invalidate that lane's evidence. What changes is only what the flag runs.

### OQ-1 and OQ-2 — **not blocking this wave**

Service accounts and token TTL are wave 2b. They are named here only so a reviewer does not look for them.

### Q-w1-1 — the resource regex must admit `purchase-hub.orders` — **docblock only, no owner decision**

§4.2.1's resource pattern is `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*(\.[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*)?$`, and the spec glosses the second dot segment as *"the two legitimate nested resources, `catalog.attributes` and `inventory.transfers`"*. The tree contains **more than three**: mechanically, the 327-key catalogue's nested resources are `batches.recall`, `catalog.attributes`, `catalog.labels`, `catalog.variants`, `fiscal.events`, `fiscal.refunds`, `inventory.adjustments`, `inventory.transfers`, `purchase-hub.orders`, `pos.tolerance`, `scheduling.appointments`, `scheduling.bays`, `settings.fiscal`, `workshop.payroll` and `workshop.technicians`. Every one of them **matches the regex as written**; only the prose gloss is short.

**This plan implements the regex unchanged** and replaces the docblock's illustrative list with the mechanically derived one, plus a registry test asserting `catalog.attributes`, `inventory.transfers` and `purchase-hub.orders` all construct through `make()`. No behaviour changes. Flagged so a gate does not read the gloss as a whitelist and reject a key wave 0b already deployed.

> **Two resource spellings do NOT match the regex, and they are `legacy()` for that reason** — `taxation.tax_configurations` and `taxation.withholding_rules` carry an underscore inside a resource segment, which `RESOURCE_PATTERN` forbids, and so do the four `pos_orders.*` keys. `make()` would throw `InvalidPermissionKey` on all six. They are declared with `legacy()`, which derives its resource by `strpos($key, '.')` and never validates the shape. Task 1.4's table marks them; a manifest that used `make()` for them would fail at the first `PermissionRegistry::definitions()` call.

### Q-w1-3 — the seeder shim's `run()` KEEPS the lane's marked-tenant preservation branch — **binding, not a preference**

`lane/w-lot-a-1a` ships a **mandatory PG preservation test**, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest.php`, whose two cases assert that a flag-off reseed on a **marked** tenant leaves the permission snapshot **byte-identical** (`assertSame($before, $this->orderedPermissionSnapshot())`) and that `manager` still lacks `batches.recall`. Spec §4.3.4's shim sketch shows `run(): void; // → $this->sync->sync($tenantId, write: true)` — and a bare delegation **reds that test**, because the first sync of a tenant necessarily writes adoption rows.

**This plan preserves the branch.** `run()` keeps the lane's control flow verbatim for the marked-tenant case: on a marked tenant with `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` off it emits the `WLOTA1A-RESEED … marked_tenant_delta_preserved` marker and **returns without delegating**, exactly as today (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:52-55,72-77`); on every other path it delegates to `PermissionSyncService::sync($tenantId, write: true)`. The enforcement branch that invokes `LotActionPermissionDelta` is **deleted** — wave 1 replaces the seeder body and nothing in it calls the delta, which is what `PermissionSyncServiceTest::sync_references_no_lot_action_symbol` asserts.

**The consequence, stated rather than hidden:** `tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` is a **no-op on a marked tenant** after wave 1, exactly as it is before it. That is harmless **because the deploy path is `permissions:sync-fleet`, not `tenants:seed`** — `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` enforces that no wave's deploy step names the seeder — and because provisioning calls the sync service directly. If the orchestrator would rather the shim delegate unconditionally, the bounded edit is: delete the preservation branch **and** obtain the lane owner's agreement to retire `RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest`, which is that lane's evidence, not this one's. **Do not delete a lane's mandatory test to make your own shim simpler.**

### Q-w1-2 — **CLOSED as an arithmetic defect: the catalogue is 327**

Rev 1 wrote `304 + 19 − 0 = 323` and asserted that the two W-LOT keys were *"inside the 304-derived set as the lane left it"*. **They are not.** Rev 2 corrected that to **325** but read the entry tree as `dev + W-LOT + 0b`, which wave 0b **rev 3** then changed: `lane/t2-receipt-spine` became a **whole-wave** entry condition of 0b, so T2's two transfer keys are in the tree before wave 1 opens (plan gate r2, BLOCKER **B2-6**). The evidence, re-derived mechanically:

- `dev:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:48-559` — `permissionNames()` returns a literal array of **304** keys, all unique;
- `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:91-94` — the lane **renames** that array to the private `legacyPermissionNames()` (still 304, byte-for-byte the same list) and makes `permissionNames()` a wrapper:
  ```php
  public static function permissionNames(): array
  {
      return array_values(array_unique([...self::legacyPermissionNames(), 'batches.recall.request', 'treasury.manage_all_locations']));
  }
  ```
  `array_diff` of the wrapper's output against `dev`'s is exactly `['batches.recall.request', 'treasury.manage_all_locations']`, and the reverse difference is empty. The lane's public catalogue is **306**;
- wave 0b adds **19** (`LegacyRoleBaseline::WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS['admin']`, 0b rev 3 — all nineteen, per its BLOCKER B0b-1 ruling);
- `lane/t2-receipt-spine` adds **2**. Its `permissionNames()` block gains `'inventory.transfers.reconcile'` and `'inventory.transfers.close'` at `lane/t2-receipt-spine:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:201-202`, immediately below the four transfer keys `dev` already carries, and the `manager` template gains both on one line at `:604` (inside the `'manager' => [` block that opens at `:587`). **No other template names them**, so `general_manager` receives them only through W-LOT's `manager + batches.recall + treasury.manage_all_locations` derivation, and `cashier`, `viewer`, `operator`, `technician` and `accountant` receive neither. Their `templateDefaults` are therefore `manager, general_manager` — **identical to the four transfer keys already in the catalogue**, which is the cross-check that this is a mechanical addition and not a judgement;
- the eleven `PermissionRenameMap` entries change spellings and collapse nothing (target and source sets are disjoint, and no target is already present), so the count is unchanged.

**306 + 19 + 2 = 327.** The parity fixture is **327 keys in their post-rename spelling**, and this plan says 327 in every count, comment, fixture and precondition. **Generate the fixture from the tree at the lane base rather than trusting even this arithmetic** (Task 1.3 Step 1 gives the exact command); if the generated count is not 327, **stop and reconcile before writing a single manifest** — a parity fixture that disagrees with the tree makes every subsequent manifest task unverifiable. In particular, **327 vs 325 is the mechanical signal that T2 did not merge**, and the answer is to merge it, not to lower the number.

## Task 1.1: `PermissionVerb` — the closed verb enum

**Thirty-two cases.** The list is derived mechanically from what the 327-key catalogue already uses **plus** the verbs the accepted T-2/T-3 spec needs. The measured histogram over the 327 keys — verb, then count of keys whose action decomposes onto it — is:

`view` 72 · `create` 39 · `manage` 32 · `update` 32 · `delete` 23 · `approve` 8 · `cancel` 7 · `post` 6 · `refund` 6 · `convert` 5 · `transfer` 4 · `adjust` 3 · `close` 3 · `confirm` 3 · `void` 3 · `complete` 2 · `export` 2 · `import` 2 · `operate` 2 · `print` 2 · `receive` 2 · `reconcile` 2 · `allocate` 1 · `assign` 1 · `correct` 1 · `pay` 1 · `reject` 1 · `request` 1 · `reverse` 1 · `submit` 1 — **thirty** verbs in use, and **`restore` and `override` are used by no key today**.

> **`close` and `reconcile` each gained one user in rev 3** (plan gate r2 **B2-6**): `lane/t2-receipt-spine` merges before this wave and brings `inventory.transfers.close` and `inventory.transfers.reconcile` with it, so `close` is 2 → **3** and `reconcile` is 1 → **2**. The enum's case list is unchanged — both verbs were already declared for exactly these keys. They are declared anyway: `restore` is the counterpart of the 23 `delete` keys and `override` is what §4.6's denial audit classifies, and a *closed* enum that omits the obvious counterpart of a verb it does contain invites the next contributor to reach for `legacy()`. `PermissionVerbTest` asserts the pair is unused **and that the assertion is a snapshot, not a rule** — a later wave declaring `products.restore` deletes that one line and nothing else.

Two cases exist for reasons a reviewer will ask about:

- **`Close` is a real case, not an alias.** It has three users once T2 merges (`fiscal-periods.close`, `pos.close_shift_with_variance`, `inventory.transfers.close`), and the accepted T-2/T-3 spec requires `inventory.transfers.reconcile` and `inventory.transfers.close` as **two independently grantable permissions** while `inventory.transfers.complete` already exists in the catalogue — mapping `.close` onto `Complete` would collide.
- **`Pay` is a case and it is financially decisive.** `expenses.pay` is a live key held by `manager`, `general_manager` and `accountant`, and §4.7 names it as a decisive half of the `expense` SoD group. Without it the key cannot be constructed by `make()` at all and cannot be classified by the SoD test.

**`confirm` is financially decisive.** It is what makes a purchase order a commitment and an order a promise — and it is the decisive half of SoD baseline rows 11 and 12, because `purchase-orders.approve` **does not exist in the catalogue** (see the note in Task 1.4).

**Files:** create `apps/api/app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/PermissionVerb.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionVerbTest.php`.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `PermissionVerbTest` first** (it fails to load: the enum does not exist). Cases: exactly 32; every value is lowercase `^[a-z]+$`; the eight decisive verbs are **exactly** `post, reverse, void, approve, confirm, refund, allocate, pay`; `view`, `print` and `export` are the only non-write verbs; `Close` and `Complete` are distinct cases with distinct values; and `restore` and `override` are the two currently-unused cases.

- [ ] **Step 2 — the enum.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Shared\Domain\Authorization;

/**
 * The CLOSED verb enum (owner ruling D5). Verbs are declared, not invented.
 *
 * Thirty-two cases: the thirty the 327-key catalogue already uses, plus
 * `restore` and `override`, plus the verbs the accepted T-2/T-3 transfer spec
 * needs (both of which now have real users, via lane/t2-receipt-spine). A key
 * whose action is not one of these is either a legacy() key
 * (which decomposes into nothing and says so) or a mistake — there is no third
 * option, and that is the point.
 */
enum PermissionVerb: string
{
    // read / write lifecycle
    case View = 'view';
    case Create = 'create';
    case Update = 'update';
    case Delete = 'delete';
    case Restore = 'restore';

    // document lifecycle
    case Post = 'post';
    case Cancel = 'cancel';
    case Reverse = 'reverse';
    case Void = 'void';
    case Correct = 'correct';
    case Complete = 'complete';
    case Close = 'close';

    // workflow
    case Request = 'request';
    case Submit = 'submit';
    case Approve = 'approve';
    case Reject = 'reject';
    case Confirm = 'confirm';
    case Convert = 'convert';

    // operations
    case Receive = 'receive';
    case Transfer = 'transfer';
    case Adjust = 'adjust';
    case Reconcile = 'reconcile';
    case Allocate = 'allocate';
    case Refund = 'refund';
    case Pay = 'pay';

    // exceptions and IO
    case Override = 'override';
    case Print = 'print';
    case Export = 'export';
    case Import = 'import';

    // administration
    case Assign = 'assign';
    case Operate = 'operate';
    case Manage = 'manage';

    /** Verbs that mutate. Drives the write half of the §4.4 ratchet and the §4.6 denial audit. */
    public function isWrite(): bool
    {
        return match ($this) {
            self::View, self::Print, self::Export => false,
            default => true,
        };
    }

    /**
     * Verbs that make a FINANCIAL FACT REAL. Drives SoD-1 (§4.7).
     *
     * `confirm` is here because it is what makes a purchase order a commitment
     * and an order a promise; omitting it would have made the SoD test silently
     * vacuous on the purchase-order group it was written for. `pay` is here
     * because paying an expense is the moment money leaves.
     */
    public function isFinanciallyDecisive(): bool
    {
        return match ($this) {
            self::Post, self::Reverse, self::Void, self::Approve,
            self::Confirm, self::Refund, self::Allocate, self::Pay => true,
            default => false,
        };
    }
}
~~~

## Task 1.2: `PermissionDefinition` and the `PermissionManifest` contract

**The key is derived, never typed.** `make()` computes `key = "{$resource}.{$verb->value}" . ($qualifier !== null ? "_{$qualifier}" : '')` and throws `InvalidPermissionKey` on a malformed resource or qualifier. **The key can never disagree with its parts**, which is the whole reason the constructor is private.

**`$module` is deliberately independent of the key prefix.** Today the UI groups by `explode('.', $name)[0]` on both sides (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php` `permissionsByModule`; `apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx:37-42`), which puts `catalog.attributes.view` under a group called `catalog` and `inventory.transfers.close` under `inventory`. Grouping by **declared module** fixes both without renaming either key.

**`$behavesAs` is a STORED readonly property, not a constructor-local.** Rev 3 of the spec accepted it as an argument to `legacy()` and then read it from a classification method that had no access to it — the object literally could not answer `isFinanciallyDecisive()` for `payments.pay-supplier`, so SoD-1's own baseline rows were unclassifiable and the SoD test could not be written at all.

**Files:** `PermissionDefinition.php`, `InvalidPermissionKey.php`, `apps/api/app/Shared/Contracts/Authorization/PermissionManifest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionDefinitionTest.php`.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `PermissionDefinitionTest` first.** Eleven cases, each pinning a property the spec argues for: the key is derived and cannot be passed; a malformed resource throws (`taxation.tax_configurations` is the real-tree example); a malformed qualifier throws; `'admin'` in `templateDefaults` throws (it is implicit); `make()` **never** sets `behavesAs` (`make_never_sets_behaves_as`); `legacy()` sets `verb` to **null** and passes `behavesAs` through; `legacy()` accepts a resource `make()` would reject (`pos_orders.view`); `isFinanciallyDecisive()` reads `verb ?? behavesAs` — assert it for `payments.pay-supplier` (`behavesAs: Pay`) and `supplier-invoices.approve-invoice-first` (`behavesAs: Approve`); `isCreateClass()` for `supplier-invoices.create-pending` (`behavesAs: Create`); `deprecate()` empties `templateDefaults` and preserves every other property; and **the three nested resources** `catalog.attributes`, `inventory.transfers`, `purchase-hub.orders` all construct through `make()` (**Q-w1-1**).

- [ ] **Step 2 — `InvalidPermissionKey`** (`final class InvalidPermissionKey extends \InvalidArgumentException {}`) **and the value object.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Shared\Domain\Authorization;

/**
 * One declared permission. The KEY IS DERIVED from its parts and can therefore
 * never disagree with them — which is why the constructor is private.
 */
final readonly class PermissionDefinition
{
    /**
     * Resource: kebab segments, optionally ONE nested dot segment.
     *
     * The catalogue's nested resources, derived mechanically from the 327 keys:
     * batches.recall, catalog.attributes, catalog.labels, catalog.variants,
     * fiscal.events, fiscal.refunds, inventory.adjustments, inventory.transfers,
     * pos.tolerance, purchase-hub.orders, scheduling.appointments,
     * scheduling.bays, settings.fiscal, workshop.payroll, workshop.technicians.
     * The list is illustrative — the PATTERN is the rule (Q-w1-1).
     */
    private const RESOURCE_PATTERN = '/^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*(\.[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*)?$/';

    /** Qualifier: snake_case only. */
    private const QUALIFIER_PATTERN = '/^[a-z0-9]+(_[a-z0-9]+)*$/';

    /**
     * @param  non-empty-string        $key             canonical key, e.g. 'purchase-orders.confirm'
     * @param  non-empty-string        $module          owning module, e.g. 'Procurement' — drives UI grouping and the
     *                                                  module gate, and is NOT derived from the key prefix
     * @param  non-empty-string        $resource        kebab-case resource
     * @param  ?PermissionVerb         $verb            NULL for a legacy() key, which does not decompose
     * @param  ?PermissionVerb         $behavesAs       STORED. null for make(); set only by legacy(), for SoD classification
     * @param  ?non-empty-string       $qualifier       snake_case narrowing of the verb, e.g. 'above_threshold'
     * @param  non-empty-string        $labelKey        i18n stem, always 'permissions.actions.<action>'. Keyed by
     *                                                  module+action ONLY, so resources sharing a verb share one generic
     *                                                  label. Intentional: it is what RolesPage.tsx already does, and a
     *                                                  per-permission label would be 327 strings x 3 locales to say
     *                                                  "Create" 39 times. A resource needing distinct wording carries a
     *                                                  $qualifier, which produces its own action key.
     * @param  list<non-empty-string>  $templateDefaults  system-role template keys that receive this by default.
     *                                                    'admin' is IMPLICIT and MUST NOT be listed.
     * @param  ?non-empty-string       $sodGroup        separation-of-duties group (§4.7)
     * @param  non-empty-string        $introducedIn    release tag, e.g. '2026.09'
     * @param  ?non-empty-string       $replacedBy      successor key when $deprecated is true
     */
    private function __construct(
        public string $key,
        public string $module,
        public string $resource,
        public ?PermissionVerb $verb,
        public ?PermissionVerb $behavesAs,
        public ?string $qualifier,
        public string $labelKey,
        public string $descriptionKey,
        public array $templateDefaults,
        public ?string $sodGroup,
        public string $introducedIn,
        public bool $deprecated,
        public ?string $replacedBy,
        public bool $legacyAction,
    ) {}

    /**
     * @param  list<non-empty-string>  $templateDefaults
     */
    public static function make(
        string $module,
        string $resource,
        PermissionVerb $verb,
        ?string $qualifier = null,
        array $templateDefaults = [],
        ?string $sodGroup = null,
        string $introducedIn = '2026.09',
    ): self {
        if (preg_match(self::RESOURCE_PATTERN, $resource) !== 1) {
            throw new InvalidPermissionKey("resource '{$resource}' is not kebab-case with at most one nested segment.");
        }
        if ($qualifier !== null && preg_match(self::QUALIFIER_PATTERN, $qualifier) !== 1) {
            throw new InvalidPermissionKey("qualifier '{$qualifier}' is not snake_case.");
        }
        if (in_array('admin', $templateDefaults, true)) {
            throw new InvalidPermissionKey("'admin' is implicit and must not appear in templateDefaults for '{$resource}'.");
        }

        $action = $verb->value.($qualifier !== null ? '_'.$qualifier : '');

        return new self(
            key: $resource.'.'.$action,
            module: $module,
            resource: $resource,
            verb: $verb,
            behavesAs: null,   // a decomposing key needs no shadow verb and MUST NOT carry one
            qualifier: $qualifier,
            labelKey: 'permissions.actions.'.$action,
            descriptionKey: 'permissions.descriptions.'.$action,
            templateDefaults: array_values($templateDefaults),
            sodGroup: $sodGroup,
            introducedIn: $introducedIn,
            deprecated: false,
            replacedBy: null,
            legacyAction: false,
        );
    }

    /**
     * Escape hatch for the 59 pre-existing keys that do not decompose into
     * verb[_qualifier] — either because the action is not a closed verb
     * ('batches.traceability', 'pricing.sell_below_cost', 'reports.operational')
     * or because the RESOURCE itself is not kebab-case ('pos_orders.view',
     * 'taxation.tax_configurations.manage'). Every call is counted against a
     * SHRINK-ONLY baseline (LEGACY_ACTION_CEILING = 59); a NEW module may not
     * use it at all, because the baseline is keyed by permission key and a key
     * absent from it is growth regardless of the ceiling.
     *
     * @param  list<non-empty-string>  $templateDefaults
     * @param  ?PermissionVerb         $behavesAs  SoD classification only; REQUIRED when $sodGroup is set
     */
    public static function legacy(
        string $key,
        string $module,
        array $templateDefaults = [],
        ?string $sodGroup = null,
        string $introducedIn = '2026.09',
        ?PermissionVerb $behavesAs = null,
    ): self {
        $dot = strpos($key, '.');
        if ($dot === false) {
            throw new InvalidPermissionKey("legacy key '{$key}' has no resource segment.");
        }
        if (in_array('admin', $templateDefaults, true)) {
            throw new InvalidPermissionKey("'admin' is implicit and must not appear in templateDefaults for '{$key}'.");
        }

        $resource = substr($key, 0, $dot);
        $action = substr($key, $dot + 1);

        return new self(
            key: $key,
            module: $module,
            resource: $resource,
            verb: null,        // a key like batches.traceability genuinely HAS no verb from the closed enum
            behavesAs: $behavesAs,
            qualifier: null,
            labelKey: 'permissions.actions.'.$action,
            descriptionKey: 'permissions.descriptions.'.$action,
            templateDefaults: array_values($templateDefaults),
            sodGroup: $sodGroup,
            introducedIn: $introducedIn,
            deprecated: false,
            replacedBy: null,
            legacyAction: true,
        );
    }

    public static function deprecate(self $definition, ?string $replacedBy): self
    {
        return new self(
            key: $definition->key,
            module: $definition->module,
            resource: $definition->resource,
            verb: $definition->verb,
            behavesAs: $definition->behavesAs,
            qualifier: $definition->qualifier,
            labelKey: $definition->labelKey,
            descriptionKey: $definition->descriptionKey,
            // A deprecated key leaves EVERY template. Step 6's exact diff then
            // revokes it from every uncustomised template-linked role.
            templateDefaults: [],
            sodGroup: $definition->sodGroup,
            introducedIn: $definition->introducedIn,
            deprecated: true,
            replacedBy: $replacedBy,
            legacyAction: $definition->legacyAction,
        );
    }

    /** verb + optional '_' . qualifier, or the legacy suffix. */
    public function action(): string
    {
        if ($this->verb === null) {
            $dot = strpos($this->key, '.');

            return $dot === false ? $this->key : substr($this->key, $dot + 1);
        }

        return $this->verb->value.($this->qualifier !== null ? '_'.$this->qualifier : '');
    }

    /** Reads the STORED pair — this is why $behavesAs is a constructor property. */
    public function isFinanciallyDecisive(): bool
    {
        return ($this->verb ?? $this->behavesAs)?->isFinanciallyDecisive() === true;
    }

    /** The create-class half of SoD-1, on the same stored pair. */
    public function isCreateClass(): bool
    {
        return ($this->verb ?? $this->behavesAs) === PermissionVerb::Create;
    }

    public function isWrite(): bool
    {
        return ($this->verb ?? $this->behavesAs)?->isWrite() === true;
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Shared\Contracts\Authorization;

use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionDefinition;

/**
 * A module's permission declaration. It lives in the module's DOMAIN layer
 * because it is a pure declaration with no infrastructure dependency, and other
 * modules never import it — they read the registry through this Shared contract,
 * which is the only sanctioned cross-module channel (rule 6).
 */
interface PermissionManifest
{
    /** The module name every definition below must declare. */
    public function module(): string;

    /** @return list<PermissionDefinition> */
    public function definitions(): array;
}
~~~

## Task 1.3: `PermissionRegistry`, the tagging wiring, the two fixtures, and the green-per-commit machinery

**Tagging, not a hard-coded array.** Each module's existing service provider contributes its manifest with **one line in that module**:
```php
$this->app->tag([ProcurementPermissionManifest::class], 'permission.manifests');
```
and the registry resolves `$app->tagged('permission.manifests')`. That is the automatic-inclusion guarantee (§3.3) at the wiring level.

**This task also builds the mechanism that keeps Task 1.4's nine commits green** (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B1-2). Rev 1 required *full-registry parity after every module group* and shipped `PermissionRegistryCoverageTest` **before** the manifests existed — both are red until the last manifest lands, and rev 1's own Global Constraints forbid a knowingly-red commit. The chosen mechanism, implemented in full below, is the **shrink-only unassigned-keys fixture**:

> `tests/Fixtures/Authorization/permission-catalogue-unassigned.json` lists the catalogue keys **not yet** carried by a manifest. It starts as all **327** in this commit and is regenerated, smaller, in each of Task 1.4's nine commits. `PermissionCatalogueMigrationProgressTest` asserts, at every commit, three things that are all true from commit one:
> 1. `registry->keys()` and the unassigned set are **disjoint**;
> 2. their **union is exactly** the 327-key parity fixture — so a manifest cannot invent a key, and a key cannot be lost;
> 3. `count(unassigned) <= UNASSIGNED_CEILING`, a constant lowered in each Task-1.4 commit and reaching **0** in `Phase 1.4i`.
>
> `PermissionRegistryCoverageTest` is driven off the same pair rather than off the parity baseline alone: **a module owning at least one key that is NOT in the unassigned set must have a tagged manifest**. That is vacuously true with zero manifests and becomes the full check when the unassigned set empties — so it ships here, green, and deleting a tag line fails **here** with the module named, from `Phase 1.4a` onward.
>
> `Phase 1.4i` — the commit that empties the set — **deletes the progress test and the unassigned fixture and adds `PermissionCatalogueParityTest`**, the exact equality assertion, in the same commit. Task 1.19 then deletes that too. There is never a commit in which the catalogue is unguarded and never one in which a guard is red.

**`validate()` runs only in console and test contexts.** A malformed catalogue must fail the build and the deploy, **not** add a per-request cost to production traffic.

**Files:** `PermissionRegistry.php`, `InvalidPermissionCatalogue.php`, `apps/api/config/permission_catalogue.php`, `apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php`, `tests/Fixtures/Authorization/permission-catalogue-parity.json`, `tests/Fixtures/Authorization/permission-catalogue-unassigned.json`, `tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionRegistryConsistencyTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionRegistryCoverageTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Authorization/PermissionCatalogueMigrationProgressTest.php`.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 2 — the registry.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Shared\Domain\Authorization;

use App\Shared\Contracts\Authorization\PermissionManifest;

final class PermissionRegistry
{
    /** @var list<PermissionDefinition>|null */
    private ?array $definitions = null;

    /** @param list<PermissionManifest> $manifests */
    public function __construct(private readonly array $manifests) {}

    /** Ordered by module, then resource, then action — DETERMINISTIC. @return list<PermissionDefinition> */
    public function definitions(): array
    {
        if ($this->definitions !== null) {
            return $this->definitions;
        }

        $all = [];
        foreach ($this->manifests as $manifest) {
            foreach ($manifest->definitions() as $definition) {
                if ($definition->module !== $manifest->module()) {
                    throw new InvalidPermissionCatalogue(sprintf(
                        "definition '%s' declares module '%s' but lives in %s's manifest.",
                        $definition->key,
                        $definition->module,
                        $manifest->module(),
                    ));
                }
                $all[] = $definition;
            }
        }

        usort($all, static fn (PermissionDefinition $a, PermissionDefinition $b): int => [$a->module, $a->resource, $a->action()] <=> [$b->module, $b->resource, $b->action()]);

        return $this->definitions = $all;
    }

    /** Includes deprecated keys. @return list<non-empty-string> */
    public function keys(): array
    {
        return array_values(array_map(static fn (PermissionDefinition $d): string => $d->key, $this->definitions()));
    }

    /** Excludes deprecated. @return list<non-empty-string> */
    public function activeKeys(): array
    {
        return array_values(array_map(
            static fn (PermissionDefinition $d): string => $d->key,
            array_filter($this->definitions(), static fn (PermissionDefinition $d): bool => ! $d->deprecated),
        ));
    }

    public function get(string $key): ?PermissionDefinition
    {
        foreach ($this->definitions() as $definition) {
            if ($definition->key === $key) {
                return $definition;
            }
        }

        return null;
    }

    /** @return array<non-empty-string, list<non-empty-string>> module => keys */
    public function byModule(): array
    {
        $grouped = [];
        foreach ($this->definitions() as $definition) {
            $grouped[$definition->module][] = $definition->key;
        }

        return $grouped;
    }

    /**
     * templateKey => keys. 'admin' is activeKeys() by construction; every other
     * template is the union of the definitions naming it.
     *
     * @return array<non-empty-string, list<non-empty-string>>
     */
    public function templateGrants(): array
    {
        $grants = ['admin' => $this->activeKeys()];
        foreach ($this->definitions() as $definition) {
            if ($definition->deprecated) {
                continue;   // a deprecated key leaves every template
            }
            foreach ($definition->templateDefaults as $template) {
                $grants[$template][] = $definition->key;
            }
        }
        foreach ($grants as $template => $keys) {
            $unique = array_values(array_unique($keys));
            sort($unique);
            $grants[$template] = $unique;
        }

        return $grants;
    }

    /** @return array<non-empty-string, list<non-empty-string>> sodGroup => keys */
    public function sodGroups(): array
    {
        $groups = [];
        foreach ($this->definitions() as $definition) {
            if ($definition->sodGroup !== null) {
                $groups[$definition->sodGroup][] = $definition->key;
            }
        }

        return $groups;
    }

    /** The registry's catalogue version — bumped by the release process, asserted non-decreasing. */
    public function version(): int
    {
        return (int) config('permission_catalogue.version', 1);
    }

    /** @throws InvalidPermissionCatalogue on any §4.2.2 violation. */
    public function validate(): void
    {
        $seen = [];
        foreach ($this->definitions() as $definition) {
            if (isset($seen[$definition->key])) {
                throw new InvalidPermissionCatalogue("duplicate permission key '{$definition->key}'.");
            }
            $seen[$definition->key] = true;
            if ($definition->deprecated && $definition->templateDefaults !== []) {
                throw new InvalidPermissionCatalogue("deprecated key '{$definition->key}' still names templateDefaults.");
            }
            if ($definition->sodGroup !== null && $definition->verb === null && $definition->behavesAs === null) {
                throw new InvalidPermissionCatalogue("legacy key '{$definition->key}' joins SoD group '{$definition->sodGroup}' without declaring behavesAs.");
            }
        }
    }
}
~~~

- [ ] **Step 4 — `PermissionRegistryConsistencyTest`.** Ten named cases, **every one of which is green over an EMPTY registry** and becomes load-bearing as manifests land:
      - `keys_are_unique` — no key appears twice across all manifests;
      - `every_key_matches_the_frozen_shape` — `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]*)+$`;
      - **`a_bare_manage_is_never_mixed_with_crud_on_one_resource`** — a resource declares a **bare `Manage`** (verb `Manage` with **no qualifier**) **or** any of `Create`/`Update`/`Delete`, never both. **The single grandfathered exception is `settings`**, listed by name in `MANAGE_CRUD_GRANDFATHERED = ['settings']`, because `settings` carries `settings.view`, `settings.update` **and** `settings.manage` today.
        > **The "bare" qualification is a rev-2 correction and it is load-bearing.** Read as *any* `Manage`, the rule has **four** violators on the real tree, not one: `settings` (`settings.manage` + `settings.update`), `users` (`users.manage_location_access` + `users.create/update/delete`), `vehicles` (`vehicles.manage_ownership` + CRUD) and `catalog-cart` (`catalog-cart.manage_all` + `catalog-cart.create`). A *qualified* manage is a narrowing of one facet — `manage_ownership` is not "manage the vehicle", it is a distinct capability — and it is exactly what the spec's own `$qualifier` mechanism exists for. Restricting the rule to the **bare** verb makes `settings` the single exception the spec claims, mechanically, on the tree. Shipping rev 1's wording would have made this test red on three additional resources on the commit that introduced it.
        The list is **shrink-only** — an entry may be removed (by splitting the resource), never added. Retiring the `settings` exception means deciding whether `settings.manage` subsumes `settings.update`, which is an owner question for wave 3 (R-w1-5).
      - `no_manifest_declares_a_rename_source_key` — no manifest key equals any `PermissionRenameMap` `from`. This is what makes sync step 1's *"a renamed key can never re-enter"* a property rather than a hope: nothing in sync re-creates a `from`, because step 2 creates only `registry->keys()`.
      - `rename_targets_are_not_sources` — 0b's assertion, re-run **against the registry** as well as against the map, so a manifest cannot introduce a chain by declaring a key that is a rename target of another entry;
      - `admin_is_never_listed_in_template_defaults`;
      - `every_module_enum_is_a_subset_of_its_manifest` (Task 1.5's `PermissionEnumManifestParityTest` owns set equality; this case is the cheap early signal, and it is vacuous until 1.5);
      - `legacy_actions_are_within_the_shrink_only_ceiling` — every `legacy()` key is in `permission-legacy-action-baseline.json`, and the count is `<= LEGACY_ACTION_CEILING = 59`. **A key absent from the baseline is growth and fails regardless of the ceiling**, which is what lets this wave land without renaming 59 keys and still close the door behind itself. The baseline file is written in `Phase 1.4i` (the last manifest commit) from the registry; until then the case skips on a missing file with an explicit message naming `Phase 1.4i`.
      - `a_legacy_key_in_a_sod_group_declares_behaves_as`;
      - `the_three_nested_resources_construct` — `catalog.attributes`, `inventory.transfers`, `purchase-hub.orders` (**Q-w1-1**).

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Authorization;

use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * TRANSITIONAL — deleted in `Phase 1.4i`, the commit that empties the
 * unassigned set and replaces this test with PermissionCatalogueParityTest.
 *
 * It exists so every one of Task 1.4's nine commits is GREEN: the registry may
 * hold a PREFIX of the catalogue, never a key outside it, and never lose one.
 */
final class PermissionCatalogueMigrationProgressTest extends TestCase
{
    /** Lowered in EVERY Phase 1.4 commit: 327 → 286 → 265 → 236 → 213 → 188 → 153 → 86 → 29 → 0. */
    private const UNASSIGNED_CEILING = 327;

    /** @return list<string> */
    private function fixture(string $name): array
    {
        /** @var list<string> $decoded */
        $decoded = json_decode((string) file_get_contents(base_path("tests/Fixtures/Authorization/{$name}.json")), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);
        sort($decoded);

        return $decoded;
    }

    public function test_the_registry_and_the_unassigned_set_are_disjoint(): void
    {
        $registry = $this->app->make(PermissionRegistry::class);
        $overlap = array_values(array_intersect($registry->keys(), $this->fixture('permission-catalogue-unassigned')));

        $this->assertSame([], $overlap, 'these keys are declared by a manifest AND still listed as unassigned: '.implode(', ', $overlap));
    }

    public function test_the_registry_and_the_unassigned_set_partition_the_catalogue_exactly(): void
    {
        $registry = $this->app->make(PermissionRegistry::class);
        $union = array_values(array_unique([...$registry->keys(), ...$this->fixture('permission-catalogue-unassigned')]));
        sort($union);

        $this->assertSame(
            $this->fixture('permission-catalogue-parity'),
            $union,
            'the manifests plus the unassigned list must be EXACTLY the frozen 327-key catalogue — a key was invented or lost.',
        );
    }

    public function test_the_unassigned_set_only_shrinks(): void
    {
        $unassigned = $this->fixture('permission-catalogue-unassigned');

        $this->assertLessThanOrEqual(
            self::UNASSIGNED_CEILING,
            count($unassigned),
            'the unassigned set grew; UNASSIGNED_CEILING is shrink-only and is lowered by each Phase 1.4 commit.',
        );
    }
}
~~~

## Task 1.4: migrate the 327 keys into 38 manifests — nine green commits

**This is the wave's spine, and it is mechanical.** §4.9.1's table assigns every prefix an owning module; the manifests are the table made executable. **Rev 3 supplies the complete assignment — all 327 keys, each with its constructor, verb or `behavesAs`, `templateDefaults` derived from the post-0b-and-T2 grant map, `sodGroup`, legacy flag and deprecation flag** (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B1-2; plan gate r2, BLOCKER B2-6). There is no ellipsis and no "…" anywhere below: what is printed is what is written.

**Do not delete the seeder array in this task.** It goes in Task 1.14, in the same commit that lands the shim, so the parity oracle and the array coexist while the cut-over is reviewed.

### Where `templateDefaults` comes from — and why it is not a judgement

Every key's `templateDefaults` is read **off the post-0b grant map**, role by role, with `admin` excluded because it is implicit. The map is, exactly:

```
lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:97-108   rolePermissionGrants()
  = legacyRolePermissionGrants()                                    (:658-950, the seven legacy templates)
  + $grants['admin']           = self::permissionNames()            (:100)
  + $grants['manager']         = manager − batches.recall + batches.recall.request   (:101)
  + $grants['general_manager'] = manager + batches.recall + treasury.manage_all_locations  (:102)
  + $grants[cashier|viewer|operator] += batches.view                (:104-106)
∪ LegacyRoleBaseline::WAVE_0B_ADDITIONS   (wave 0b rev 3: admin ×19, manager ×7, general_manager ×7)
∪ lane/t2-receipt-spine                   (permissionNames() :201-202 + the manager grant line :604:
                                           inventory.transfers.reconcile, inventory.transfers.close)
canonicalised through PermissionRenameMap (11 spellings)
```

**T2's two keys enter the map through `manager` and only through `manager`.** The lane grants them on one line inside the `'manager' => [` block (`lane/t2-receipt-spine:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:604`, block opening at `:587`) and nowhere else; `general_manager` receives them because W-LOT derives it as `manager + batches.recall + treasury.manage_all_locations`. So both rows carry `templateDefaults: ['manager', 'general_manager']` — **byte-identical to the four transfer keys the catalogue already had**, which is the cross-check that this is the map speaking rather than a judgement (plan gate r2, **B2-6**).

Applying that produces these template sizes, which are the arithmetic every table below is consistent with:

| Template | Keys | Note |
|---|---:|---|
| `admin` | **327** | implicit — never listed in a manifest |
| `general_manager` | 259 | `manager` + `batches.recall` + `treasury.manage_all_locations` (255 + 2 T2 keys = 257 for `manager`, +2 = 259) |
| `manager` | 257 | 255 pre-T2, **+2** for `inventory.transfers.reconcile` / `.close` |
| `accountant` | 87 | unchanged — T2 grants it neither key |
| `operator` | 58 | unchanged |
| `cashier` | 49 | unchanged |
| `viewer` | 34 | unchanged |
| `technician` | 14 | unchanged |

**A difference from that map is a FINDING, not a design choice.** If a manifest's `templateDefaults` differs from the seeder block it replaces for any Inventory or Treasury key, that is a behaviour change and the owning reviewer becomes mandatory (see the Reviewer gate).

### Two corrections to rev 1's worked example, both verified against the tree

1. **`purchase-orders.approve` DOES NOT EXIST.** The catalogue's `purchase-orders.*` keys are exactly `view`, `create`, `update`, `delete`, `confirm`, `receive`. Rev 1's worked manifest declared `PermissionDefinition::make('Procurement', 'purchase-orders', PermissionVerb::Approve, templateDefaults: [])` — a **326th key**, which would have failed `PermissionCatalogueMigrationProgressTest`'s exact-partition assertion on the commit that introduced it. It is removed. The `purchase-order` SoD group is therefore `purchase-orders.create` **versus `purchase-orders.confirm`**, which is what spec §4.7's baseline row 3 already says, and the illustrative failure message in Task 1.13 and in the PHPStan rule is updated to name `.confirm`.
2. **`inventory.transfers.reconcile` and `inventory.transfers.close` ARE declared by wave 1 — rev 3 reverses rev 2 here, and the reason is an entry-tree change, not a change of mind.** Rev 1 declared them speculatively; rev 2 removed them because the T-2/T-3 lane had not landed; **wave 0b rev 3 then made `lane/t2-receipt-spine` a whole-wave entry condition of 0b**, so by the time wave 1 opens both keys are in `permissionNames()` (`lane/t2-receipt-spine:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:201-202`) and in the `manager` template (`:604`). A wave-1 catalogue that omitted them would fail `PermissionCatalogueParityTest` against a tree that has them — the mirror image of rev 1's defect. **The `Inventory` manifest therefore declares SIX transfer keys** (`view`, `create`, `complete`, `cancel`, **`reconcile`**, **`close`**) and `InventoryPermission` has six matching cases. The two closed verbs `Reconcile` and `Close` were already declared in Task 1.1 for exactly this; what wave 1 still does **not** ship is T-2/T-3's *routes* — the two-permission AND gate that is `->middleware(['can:'.$a, 'can:'.$b])` by construction is that lane's work, and wave 1 only guarantees the keys exist and are enum-reachable (plan gate r2, **B2-6**).
   > **This is the one place where a stale entry tree silently changes the answer**, which is why Phase 0's count check says *"if it is not 327, STOP"* rather than *"record which number you saw"*.

### The module mapping — 38 modules, one manifest each

★ marks a prefix whose UI group *changes*, because grouping moves from `explode('.', $name)[0]` to the declared module.

| Key prefix(es) | Owning module |
|---|---|
| `partners.*` | Partner |
| `contacts.*` | Contact |
| `products.*`, `enrichment.*`, `composite-items.*`, `modifier-groups.*`, `categories.*` | Product |
| `catalog.attributes.*` ★, `catalog.variants.*` ★, `catalog.labels.*` ★ | Catalog |

| `workshop-bundles.*`, `work-orders.*`, `workshop.technicians.*` ★, `workshop.payroll.*` ★ | Workshop |
| `menus.*`, `menu-categories.*` | Menu |
| `promotions.*` | Promotion |
| `coupons.*` | Coupon |
| `loyalty.*` | Loyalty |
| `vehicles.*` | Vehicle |
| `documents.*`, `quotes.*`, `orders.*`, `invoices.*`, `credit-notes.*`, `deliveries.*` | Document |
| `purchase-orders.*`, `goods-receipt.*`, `supplier-invoices.*`, `purchase-quote-requests.*` | Procurement |
| `document-ingestions.*` | DocumentIngestion |
| `purchase-hub.orders.*` | PurchaseHub |
| `inventory.*`, `inventory.transfers.*` ★, `inventory.adjustments.*` ★ | Inventory |
| `replenishment.*` | Replenishment |
| `batches.*`, `batches.recall.*` | BatchExpiry — **owns `batches.recall.request`** (W-LOT-A-1a) |
| `uom.*`, `units.*` | Uom |
