## Task 1.10: `PermissionSyncService`, both commands, the dry-run sentinel — and the deletion of all eight wave-0b stopgap artefacts. **ONE commit.**

> **This is one commit by ruling** (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B1-4). Wave 0b's binding cutover promise is that the ensure files and their deploy rows disappear **in the commit that introduces their replacement**; rev 1 split them across Tasks 9, 10 and 14, and additionally had Task 10 reuse 0b's `DryRunRollback` while Task 14 deleted it with no replacement. The commit below lands the replacement and removes the stopgap atomically, and it ships wave 1's own sentinel in the same breath, so there is never a moment at which the dry-run path has no rollback carrier.

### One public method

`sync(string $tenantId, bool $write): PermissionSyncResult`. There is no `syncRole()`: with the template-migration machinery retired there is no step 5 to run for one template, and the remaining single-role work is exactly the step-6 delta for one already-adopted role — which does not need a second public entry point, a second transaction shape, or a second set of counters.

### Two phases, one plan — the rev-3 shape

**`sync()` PLANS, then executes.** Rev 2 interleaved the two: each step read the database, decided, and wrote *if `$write`*. That made the dry run a **different computation** rather than the same one with the writes suppressed — a legacy tenant's roles stayed `template_key = NULL` through a dry run, so steps 6 and 8 re-queried and found nothing, and the first fleet dry run of the wave under-reported every legacy tenant and falsely reported `admin_role_missing` on any tenant missing `general_manager` (plan gate r2, BLOCKER **B2-1**).

Rev 3 splits the method in two:

| Phase | What it does | Writes |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — plan** | `plan(string $tenantId): SyncPlan` runs all nine steps against a **virtual state**: a `list<string>` of permission names that steps 1 and 2 mutate in memory, and an `array<string, VirtualRole>` that steps 4, 5, 6 and 7 mutate in memory. It produces a `SyncPlan` — six lists of planned work — and a fully-populated `PermissionSyncResult`. | **none, on either path** |
| **2 — execute** | `execute(SyncPlan $plan, string $tenantId): void` performs exactly the work the plan names, in the same order, and **verifies the applier's own delta matches the planned one**. | only when `$write` **and** the plan is green |

Three properties follow, and each of them is a test:

1. **The dry run and the apply produce byte-identical marker lines** for the same starting state, because they are the same `plan()` call. `PermissionsSyncTest::a_dry_run_and_an_apply_report_the_same_line` asserts exactly that.
2. **A legacy tenant is adopted, and its roles created, IN THE VIRTUAL STATE**, so the dry run reports the deltas the apply would perform. `PermissionSyncAdoptionTest::a_dry_run_on_a_legacy_tenant_reports_every_delta_the_apply_performs` is the B2-1 red: against rev 2's service it reports `templates_applied=0` and `outcome=FAILED reason=admin_role_missing` on a tenant that has an `admin` but no `general_manager`.
3. **Every non-green outcome leaves the transaction by ROLLBACK.** `sync()` throws **`SyncRollback`** whenever `outcome->isGreen()` is false — on the applying path as much as the dry-run path — so a `BLOCKED` or `FAILED` apply commits nothing at all. Rev 2's `stepAdmin()` set `FAILED` and **returned**, after which the transaction settled and committed steps 1–7's writes.
4. **Every virtual grant set is CANONICAL, so the plan a legacy tenant previews is the plan its apply performs** (plan gate r3, BLOCKER **B3-2**; orchestrator ruling **[R]**). Rev 3 renamed only the virtual *permission-name* set in step 1 and then built the role projection from **live, pre-rename** relationship names — while the executor renames the `permissions` row **in place**, preserving every pivot, so `TemplateDeltaApplier` re-reads the **post**-rename names. On any tenant whose template holds a rename **source** the planner computed `+uom.update / −uom.edit` and the applier computed **nothing**, and step 6's own `plan_divergence` guard rolled the whole run back — on a tenant whose dry run had just reported green. **This is not hypothetical:** W-LOT's `manager` holds `uom.edit` and `deliveries.edit` (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:681,684`) while the wave-1 template carries `uom.update` and `deliveries.update` (`:1316,1392`), so **every ordinary legacy tenant** would have failed its first apply. Rev 4 applies **`PermissionRenameMap::canonicalise()`** — 0b's single-pass, deduplicated, sorted, order-free, idempotent map (`2f172dd5e:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-0b.md:413-433`) — to **every** virtual grant set as it enters the projection (`virtualiseRoles()`, `holdersOf()`), to the template and admin **targets** before the delta is computed, and to **both sides of the executor's divergence check**. `PermissionSyncAdoptionTest::a_legacy_tenant_holding_rename_sources_plans_the_same_delta_it_applies` is the B3-2 red.

> **A non-green plan is also EMPTY.** `SyncPlan::rejected()` carries the verdict and no work, so nothing can execute a rejected plan even by mistake. The `mutations` counters on that result describe **what was planned and refused**, not rows written — the marker's `outcome=` token is what says nothing landed, and it is the same number the dry run prints.

### The nine steps, in this order

1. **Rename.** For each `(from, to)` where a `permissions` row named `from` exists and none named `to` exists: `UPDATE permissions SET name = :to WHERE name = :from AND guard_name = 'sanctum'`. **In place** — the primary key never changes, so every `role_has_permissions` and `model_has_permissions` row survives untouched and **no grant is lost**. If **both** exist the run is `BLOCKED` with `reason=rename_target_exists` (a human must decide which grants win). If **neither** exists the entry is a no-op and is **reported as stale**, so the map can be trimmed. **A renamed key can never re-enter:** step 2 creates only `registry->keys()`, and `no_manifest_declares_a_rename_source_key` proves no manifest declares a `from`.
2. **Create.** For every `registry->keys()` **including deprecated** — a deprecated permission keeps its row for one release so a custom role referencing it does not break. The implementation is `exists()` then `Permission::create()`, **not** `firstOrCreate`, for 0b's stated reason: `firstOrCreate` cannot report which branch it took (so `created=` is uncountable) and it **writes on the dry-run path**.
3. **Orphans.** A permission name present in the **post-step-2 virtual set** and absent from the registry is **reported, never deleted**. Deleting it would cascade its grants away, and a row can legitimately be a leftover from a tenant that has not yet received a deploy that removed a module. **This step writes nothing**, which is why it may sit before adoption. Its count is a **gauge**. `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` is wave 3 and is a deliberate operator action.
   > Reading it off the **virtual** set rather than off the table is a rev-3 side effect worth naming: rev 2 computed orphans from the live `permissions` table, which on the applying path was already post-create and on the dry-run path was not, so the same tenant could report two different `orphans=` numbers depending on the mode. Over the virtual set the two are equal by construction — and equal to rev 2's applying-path number, because step 2 only ever adds registry keys.
4. **Adopt** — **the first step that touches `roles`, and it writes no grant.** In the plan it writes nothing at all: it records an adoption verdict per role and updates that role in the **virtual state** (`template_key`, `template_version = 0`, `customised`), which is what lets steps 6 and 8 behave identically on both paths. For each **existing** role whose `name` matches a `SystemRoleTemplate` case (resolved with the NULL-team-tolerant predicate) **and whose `template_key IS NULL`**: compute its current grant set, compare through `LegacyRoleBaseline::matchesVersion0()`, and adopt with `is_system = true`, `template_key = <name>`, `template_version = 0`, and `customised_at = NULL` (equal) or `customised_at = now()` (different). Counted as `adopted_pristine` / `adopted_customised`, logged per role, listed in `--dry-run`. **Adoption is one-way and once**: a role that already carries a `template_key` is never re-classified. Roles whose name matches no template stay `is_system = false` and are never touched.

   > **`customised_at = now()` for the "different" branch is deliberately the CONSERVATIVE direction.** It can only ever *withhold* a delta from a tenant that had in fact not customised, which surfaces as a visible banner and a one-click *Re-apply template*. The opposite error — adopting a genuinely customised role as pristine and then silently re-granting what the tenant removed — is precisely I-20 in a new costume.

5. **Create missing template roles.** For each `SystemRoleTemplate` case with no resolvable role in this tenant, **plan** a creation — and put the role into the **virtual state** with no grants and `template_version = <registry version>`, so step 6 computes its full grant delta and step 8 finds it on the dry-run path too. Execution creates it with `tenant_id = :tenantId`, `guard_name = 'sanctum'`, `is_system = true`, `template_key`, `template_version = <registry version>`, `customised_at = NULL`, and **no grants yet** — step 6 grants. Counted as `roles_created`. **`general_manager` is created here like any other template role, with one addition:** when `roleMarkers->tenantCarriesMarker('w-lot-a-1a')` is `false`, the insert **also** sets `provisioning_source = 'w-lot-a-1a'` and `marker_written` becomes 1. Every clause of the lane's CHECK holds by construction on this path — sync always sets a tenant id and always this name and guard — so the write cannot violate it, and the partial unique is satisfied because sync reaches this branch only when the tenant has no marked role.
6. **Template deltas — an EXACT diff on uncustomised template-linked roles, over CANONICAL sets.** The **locked read of the template roles happens once, in `resolveTemplateRoles()`** (`FOR UPDATE`, `roles.name` ASC then `roles.id` ASC), and everything after it reads the virtual state built from that read — so the row order and the lock are exactly wave 0b's and there is no second, unordered read of the **template** roles. (Two other statements do touch `roles`, and neither weakens that: `refuseTemplateKeyNameMismatch()` is a single scalar `value()` probe that takes **no lock** and reads **no grants**, and step 7's `holdersOf()` takes `FOR UPDATE` in the **same** `name`-then-`id` order over a different, deprecation-scoped row set.) **Both sides of the diff are canonicalised** — the projection on the way in, the template target here — so a template naming a rename target and a tenant holding its source produce the **same** delta at plan time and at apply time (plan gate r3, **B3-2**). For each **non-`admin`** template: **skip** the role entirely if its virtual `customised` flag is true (counted in the `templates_skipped_customised` **gauge**, `template_version` left where it was, which is what lets the Roles UI say *"this role is 3 versions behind"*); otherwise plan the exact `added` / `removed` / `versionChanged` triple against `registry->templateGrants()`. Execution then runs the **one** `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo()` per planned delta and **asserts the applier's returned `TemplateDelta` equals the planned one** — a mismatch is `FAILED reason=plan_divergence:<template>` and rolls the whole run back, because a planner whose numbers can disagree with the writer's is worse than no planner. One **`RoleSyncedV1`** is emitted per role whose grant set actually changed.
7. **Deprecation replacement grants** — **the one case in which sync writes a custom role.** For every deprecated definition naming a `replacedBy`, grant the successor to every role currently holding the deprecated key, **custom roles included**, and emit a `RoleSyncedV1` per affected role. A deprecation with `replacedBy: null` writes nothing anywhere — **and all 22 of wave 1's deprecations carry `replacedBy: null`, so this step is a provable no-op in this wave and exists for the next one.** The reason it is not a carve-out: a deprecated key stops being granted by any template and will eventually be pruned, so a custom role not given the successor **loses the capability on prune day, silently**, which is exactly what D2 forbids.
8. **Admin.** The role whose `template_key = 'admin'` is set to exactly `registry->activeKeys()` with `syncPermissions()`. This is the one place a full replace is correct, and it is what replaces `Permission::all()` — after it, the catalogue is once again the boundary of what `admin` holds, and a stray row inserted out of band is no longer silently attached. Counted as `admin_changes`, **including removals**.

   **`admin_role_missing` has TWO homes in rev 3, and only the second is reachable** (plan gate r2, B2-1):
   - a **planning invariant** — the virtual state carries no `admin` after step 5. In `db_per_tenant` mode step 5 plans an `admin` whenever one is absent, so this fires only if `SystemRoleTemplate::cases()` stops containing `Admin`, which `SystemRoleTemplateAlignmentTest` already forbids. It is kept because a planner that silently produced no admin plan would be worse than one that refuses;
   - a **post-apply verification**, `verifyAdmin()`, run inside the transaction **after** `execute()`: re-read the admin role under the lock and refuse if it is **absent** (`reason=admin_role_missing`) or if its grant set is not **exactly** `activeKeys()` (`reason=admin_not_current`). Either verdict makes the outcome non-green, and PHASE 3 then throws `SyncRollback`, so **the whole run — steps 1 through 7 included — rolls back.** This is the wave-1 home of 0b's `AdminRoleMissing` sentinel, which is deleted with the rest of the stopgap, and it is the failure B2-1 named: *"if that failure becomes reachable on apply through a concurrent or inconsistent state, prior permission, adoption and template writes commit despite the promised zero-write failure."*
9. **Cache — AFTER commit, with the team set INSIDE the callback.** `DB::afterCommit(…)`, registered **inside** the transaction and **only on a green applying run** (a dry run writes nothing to flush, and a rolled-back run must not flush a snapshot it did not change), so it fires once the commit has landed. A `finally` inside the transaction callback executes **before** Laravel commits, leaving a window in which another request rebuilds and re-caches the *old* snapshot which then outlives the commit — which is the defect this step's ordering exists to prevent.

   **The callback captures the tenant id and sets/restores the permissions team ITSELF** (plan gate r3, BLOCKER **B3-5**; orchestrator ruling **[R]**). The S-1 key scheme derives the cache namespace from the team id, and rev 3 relied on `sync()`'s outer scope still holding it when the callback fired. That holds **only when this service opens the root transaction.** Laravel runs after-commit callbacks only at level zero (`afterCommitCallbacksShouldBeExecuted($level) { return $level === 0; }`, `/Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/apps/api/vendor/laravel/framework/src/Illuminate/Database/DatabaseTransactionsManager.php:243-246`, from `commit()` at `:75-78`), and the **compatibility registration path already wraps tenant initialization in an outer transaction** — `DB::transaction(function () …` at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/AuthController.php:362`, with `initializeForNewRegistration()` inside it at `:488-492`, and that service is one of sync's **two declared triggers**. On that path the inner transaction returns, `sync()`'s `finally` restores the **previous** team, and only later does the outer commit run the flush — against the wrong namespace, leaving the new tenant's stale snapshot cached. `PermissionsSyncTest::the_cache_is_flushed_for_this_tenant_under_a_nested_outer_transaction` is the B3-5 red; the second-connection test alone does **not** reach this case, because it never nests.

### The result marker

```
PERMISSIONS-SYNC tenant=<uuid> mode=APPLY|DRY_RUN outcome=APPLIED|ALREADY_CURRENT|BLOCKED|FAILED
                 mutations: renamed=<n> created=<n> adopted_pristine=<n> adopted_customised=<n>
                            roles_created=<n> templates_applied=<n> templates_removed=<n>
                            replacements_granted=<n> admin_changes=<n> marker_written=<n>
                 gauges:    orphans=<n> templates_skipped_customised=<n>
                 reason=<token>
```

**Two kinds of number, and only one of them can be zero on a re-run.** The ten **`mutations`** counters are rows this run **wrote** — all zero, always, on a re-run. The two **`gauges`** are state this run **observed** — **unconstrained**; on a tenant with one customised `manager` and one orphan row, `orphans=1 templates_skipped_customised=1` is reported on **every** run forever, and that is not a failure. `outcome=ALREADY_CURRENT` is defined as *every `mutations` counter zero **AND** zero database writes*, so the marker's outcome token and its numbers cannot disagree. `markerLine(string $tenantId, bool $write)` renders `mode=` itself, so the per-tenant command and the fleet command emit **one** shape — 0b rev 2's minor fix, adopted.

**Why the second run is `ALREADY_CURRENT` — re-derived, not asserted.** **1** every `from` is gone and nothing re-created it → `renamed=0`; **2** every key exists → `created=0`; **3** the orphan set is a **read** and its count is a gauge; **4** every template-named role carries a `template_key` → `adopted_*=0`; **5** every case resolves → `roles_created=0`, `marker_written=0`; **6** every uncustomised template role already **equals** its template **and already carries the current `template_version`, so the conditional write does not fire** → `templates_applied=0`, `templates_removed=0`, while `templates_skipped_customised` reports the same non-zero number as the first run whenever the tenant has a customised role; **7** every role holding a deprecated key already holds its successor (vacuously, in this wave) → `replacements_granted=0`; **8** `syncPermissions(activeKeys())` on a role that already equals that set writes no pivot row → `admin_changes=0`; **9** the flush is unconditional and is not a counter.

**Exit codes:** `0` applied or already-current, `1` failed (**nothing written** — the whole run is one transaction), `2` blocked (a rename collision, an ambiguous legacy role, or one of compatibility mode's three refusals: `--tenant` with a shared table, `--compat` under database-per-tenant, or neither flag in compatibility mode).

**Compatibility mode has ONE executable command, and it is `permissions:sync --compat`** (plan gate r3, BLOCKER **B3-6**; orchestrator ruling **[R]**). With `config('tenancy_resolver.db_per_tenant') === false` the roles table is shared, so:

- `permissions:sync --tenant=<x>` is **refused** (exit 2, `reason=compatibility_mode_is_global`) — a tenant selector cannot mean anything against one shared table;
- `permissions:sync` with **no** flag is **refused** (exit 2, `reason=compat_requires_flag`) and the message names `--compat`. Rev 3 left this case falling through to `no_tenant_context` (exit 1) because a central Artisan process has no Spatie permissions team — an operator with **no** way to run the documented migration;
- `permissions:sync-fleet` is **refused** (exit 2, `reason=compatibility_mode_has_no_fleet`) — 0b's runner requires each tenant's physical database to exist, and this mode is precisely the one in which they do not;
- **`permissions:sync --compat`** runs **steps 1–4 only**, **once**, **globally**, from **central context**: no `tenancy()->initialize()`, no `Tenant` row resolved, no readiness predicate, and **no permissions team set** — so the shared table's **NULL-team** roles are the rows step 4 classifies. It reports `templates_applied=0 templates_removed=0 roles_created=0 replacements_granted=0 admin_changes=0 marker_written=0` by construction, and its marker's scope token is the reserved `tenant=compat`. `--compat` is itself **refused** (exit 2, `reason=compat_flag_in_db_per_tenant_mode`) under database-per-tenant, so the two topologies cannot be confused in either direction.

**Adoption runs there too** — it is a DB-level classification of `roles` rows, not a template application, and skipping it would leave every system role at `is_system = false`, which is exactly the state wave 2's rename/delete protection and both floors depend on. **Step 5 in particular must NOT run there**: creating one tenant's `general_manager` in a shared `roles` table provisions it for everybody, and writing the marker there would additionally claim, falsely, that every tenant in the schema carries the lane's end state. This is **stated rather than silently degraded**, because a silent degrade is exactly how I-20 happened — and rev 4's point is that "stated" now also means **executable**, with a test that runs the command rather than a paragraph promising the behaviour. Compatibility mode is the **non-production topology** and is deliberately absent from `permission-deploy-runbook.yaml`; the full contract table is under Task 1.10 Step 3.

### Step 1 — the outcome enum, the sentinel and the result DTO

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums;

enum PermissionSyncOutcome: string
{
    case Applied = 'APPLIED';
    case AlreadyCurrent = 'ALREADY_CURRENT';
    case Blocked = 'BLOCKED';
    case Failed = 'FAILED';

    public function isGreen(): bool
    {
        return $this === self::Applied || $this === self::AlreadyCurrent;
    }

    /** 0 green, 1 failed, 2 blocked — identical on the applying and the dry-run path. */
    public function exitCode(): int
    {
        return match ($this) {
            self::Applied, self::AlreadyCurrent => 0,
            self::Failed => 1,
            self::Blocked => 2,
        };
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\PermissionSyncResult;
use RuntimeException;

/**
 * WAVE 1's rollback sentinel — the replacement for wave 0b's DryRunRollback,
 * shipped in the SAME commit that deletes it (plan gate r1, BLOCKER B1-4).
 *
 * RENAMED from rev 2's SyncDryRunRollback. Rev 2's own docblock said "if a later
 * wave adds a second blocked cause, rename it to SyncRollback and update both
 * call sites"; plan gate r2's BLOCKER B2-1 adds one round later, not one wave —
 * so the rename happens here rather than being deferred into a class whose name
 * would then lie about three of its four callers.
 *
 * It is thrown from inside the transaction in every case in which the
 * transaction must NOT commit, and it carries the computed classification out:
 *
 *   1. a DRY RUN, always — the run is write-free by construction, and the
 *      rollback is the belt that makes "write-free" a property of the database
 *      rather than of the code's discipline;
 *   2. an APPLYING run whose plan is BLOCKED (a rename collision);
 *   3. an APPLYING run whose plan is FAILED, or whose post-apply admin
 *      verification fails — the B2-1 case. Rev 2 set FAILED and RETURNED, after
 *      which the transaction settled and committed steps 1-7's writes;
 *   4. an APPLYING run in which the applier's TemplateDelta diverges from the
 *      planned one (reason=plan_divergence:<template>).
 *
 * A dry run is WRITE-free, not FAILURE-free: the reported outcome, reason and
 * counters are exactly what an applying run would have produced — including
 * BLOCKED (exit 2) and FAILED (exit 1).
 */
final class SyncRollback extends RuntimeException
{
    public function __construct(public readonly PermissionSyncResult $result)
    {
        parent::__construct('sync: rolling back ('.$this->result->outcome->value.' '.$this->result->reason.')');
    }
}
~~~

**The four plan DTOs.** They exist so `SyncPlan` is strictly typed end to end — no `mixed`, no untyped arrays — and so a reviewer can read what a run *intends* without reading how it writes.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs;

use LogicException;

/**
 * One template role, as the PLANNER projects it. Immutable: every step returns a
 * NEW instance, so a step that forgets to store its result cannot silently
 * mutate the state a later step reads.
 *
 * `id` is null for a role step 5 plans to create — it has no database identity
 * until execution. `templateKey` is null for a legacy role step 4 has not yet
 * adopted, which is precisely the state rev 2's dry run could not represent.
 */
final readonly class VirtualRole
{
    /** @param list<string> $grants */
    private function __construct(
        public ?int $id,
        public string $name,
        public ?string $templateKey,
        public int $templateVersion,
        public bool $customised,
        public array $grants,
        public bool $isNew,
    ) {}

    /**
     * A role that EXISTS, as the single locked read found it.
     *
     * @param  list<string>  $grants
     */
    public static function existing(int $id, string $name, ?string $templateKey, int $templateVersion, bool $customised, array $grants): self
    {
        return new self($id, $name, $templateKey, $templateVersion, $customised, $grants, false);
    }

    /** A role step 5 PLANS to create: no id yet, no grants yet, already at the registry version. */
    public static function planned(string $name, int $templateVersion): self
    {
        return new self(null, $name, $name, $templateVersion, false, [], true);
    }

    /** Step 4's verdict in the virtual state: adopted at template_version 0, customised or not. */
    public function adopted(string $templateKey, bool $customised): self
    {
        return new self($this->id, $this->name, $templateKey, 0, $customised, $this->grants, $this->isNew);
    }

    /**
     * Step 6's verdict in the virtual state: the grant set becomes the template's.
     *
     * @param  list<string>  $grants
     */
    public function withGrants(array $grants, int $templateVersion): self
    {
        sort($grants);

        return new self($this->id, $this->name, $this->templateKey, $templateVersion, $this->customised, array_values($grants), $this->isNew);
    }

    /** Step 7's verdict in the virtual state: one replacement key added. */
    public function plus(string $key): self
    {
        $grants = $this->grants;
        $grants[] = $key;
        sort($grants);

        return new self($this->id, $this->name, $this->templateKey, $this->templateVersion, $this->customised, array_values($grants), $this->isNew);
    }

    /** Step 4 only ever adopts a role that already exists, so its id is never null there. */
    public function requireId(): int
    {
        if ($this->id === null) {
            throw new LogicException("virtual role '{$this->name}' has no database id yet.");
        }

        return $this->id;
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs;

/** One planned step-6 delta. `roleId` is null when step 5 will create the role. */
final readonly class PlannedTemplateDelta
{
    /**
     * @param  list<string>  $added
     * @param  list<string>  $removed
     */
    public function __construct(
        public ?int $roleId,
        public string $roleName,
        public string $templateKey,
        public array $added,
        public array $removed,
        public int $fromVersion,
        public int $toVersion,
        public bool $versionChanged,
    ) {}
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs;

/**
 * One planned step-7 replacement grant. The holder may be a CUSTOM role — that
 * is the whole point of step 7 — so `templateKey` and `templateVersion` are
 * nullable and are carried only to populate the RoleSyncedV1 event.
 */
final readonly class PlannedReplacement
{
    public function __construct(
        public int $roleId,
        public string $roleName,
        public ?string $templateKey,
        public ?int $templateVersion,
        public string $deprecatedKey,
        public string $replacementKey,
    ) {}
~~~
