---

## Task 1.16: the static guards

### 16a — `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` (PHPStan)

Flags **any** dotted lowercase string literal matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]*)+$` when it appears as an argument to `->can()`, `->cant()`, `->hasPermissionTo()`, `->hasAnyPermission()`, `Gate::authorize()`, `Gate::allows()`, `Gate::denies()`, or inside a `->middleware([...])` / `middleware('can:…')` / `middleware('require.any.permission:…')` string.

**Two error classes, and the second is the important one.** Flagging only literals *already in* the registry lets the single most damaging mistake through: a typo such as `invoices.posst` is absent from the registry and would therefore **pass** — while at runtime Spatie swallows `PermissionDoesNotExist`, so the route **silently 403s for everyone including `admin`**.

| Literal | Verdict | Message |
|---|---|---|
| in the registry | **error**, use the enum | *"permission literal 'purchase-orders.confirm' — use `ProcurementPermission::PurchaseOrdersConfirm->value`."* |
| **not** in the registry | **error**, unknown key | *"unknown permission literal 'invoices.posst' — it is in no PermissionManifest, so this gate denies every caller including admin. Declare it, or fix the typo."* |

**Restricting the shape match to authorization CALL SITES** (rather than to any dotted string anywhere) is what keeps the rule free of false positives on config and i18n keys, so the registry lookup is no longer load-bearing for precision.

**The rule reads the registry WITHOUT booting the application.** PHPStan runs in its own process and a rule that calls `app()` inside `processNode()` is a rule that boots Laravel per file. Instead the rule takes a **generated key list** — `apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json`, written by `permissions:export-frontend-map` in Task 1.17 and regenerated by the preflight drift guard — injected through the PHPStan service definition:
```neon
services:
    -
        class: App\PHPStan\Rules\ForbidPermissionStringLiteral
        arguments:
            projectRoot: %currentWorkingDirectory%
            keyFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json
            baselineFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json
        tags:
            - phpstan.rules.rule
```
If the key file is missing the rule reports **one** error on the first file it sees — *"permission-registry-keys.json is missing; run `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map`"* — rather than silently passing every literal.

> **`projectRoot` is how a rule learns the analysed root, and it is what makes the generated baselines usable at all** (plan gate r3, BLOCKER **B3-4**). PHPStan **absolutizes** every analysed path before analysis — `$paths = array_map(static fn(string $path): string => $currentWorkingDirectoryFileHelper->normalizePath($currentWorkingDirectoryFileHelper->absolutizePath($path)), $paths);` in the installed `phpstan.phar`'s `src/Command/CommandHelper.php:194` — and those absolute paths are what `Scope::getFile()` returns. Rev 3's rules keyed their sites on `$scope->getFile()` **raw**, while `tools/generate-permission-baselines.php` deliberately strips `getcwd().'/'` and writes **relative** paths. The baseline therefore contained `app/…:line[:key]` and the rules tested `/Users/…/apps/api/app/…:line[:key]`: **not one** pre-existing site was ever absorbed, and Task 1.16 Step 6's required whole-`app/` run was red by construction on 600-odd sites. There is no `%rootDir%`-style value a rule can read on its own — a PHPStan rule has no ambient knowledge of the project root — so it is **injected as a constructor argument from `phpstan.neon`'s `%currentWorkingDirectory%`**, which is the same `getcwd()` the generator strips because both commands run from `apps/api`. Both rules gain the parameter and the identical `relativePath()` helper, and `ForbidPermissionStringLiteralTest` asserts the round trip by **loading a generated baseline entry** rather than a hand-written path.

> **The fixture is GENERATED AND COMMITTED IN THIS TASK'S OWN COMMIT** (plan gate r2, BLOCKER **B2-4**). Rev 2 registered the rule in `Phase 1.16` and first wrote the file in `Phase 1.17`, so forward history was **red between the two commits** — the rule errors deliberately on a missing key file, and `Phase 1.16` runs whole-app PHPStan — contrary to the global green-per-commit rule. **The chosen resolution is "generate it here", not "renumber"**: the tasks keep their numbers, and Task 1.17's exporter **regenerates the same file and asserts byte-identity**, which turns the duplication into a determinism check rather than a maintenance cost.
>
> Task 1.14 has already turned `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`'s statics into registry shims by the time this task runs, so the registry is fully populated and one tinker line produces the file:
> ```
> cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api && php artisan tinker --execute='
>   $keys = app(\App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry::class)->keys();
>   sort($keys);
>   file_put_contents("tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json", json_encode($keys, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT|JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES|JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE)."\n");
>   echo count($keys), PHP_EOL;'
> ```
> **Expected: 327.** (The `app()` call is inside a `tinker --execute` one-liner, not production code — the Global Constraint's prohibition is on `app()` in classes that have a constructor to inject into.)

**Allowed call sites, encoded in the rule as PROJECT-ROOT-ANCHORED paths, never as suffix fragments:** `app/Modules/*/Domain/Authorization/*PermissionManifest.php`, `app/Modules/*/Domain/Enums/*Permission.php`, `app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/*`, `database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php` (the shim), `app/Console/Commands/SyncPermissions.php`, `app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php`, `app/Console/Commands/ReportDeadPermissions.php`, and everything under `tests/`.

> **The exemption is by path, not by filename suffix** (plan gate r3, MAJOR **M3-2**; orchestrator ruling **[R]**). Rev 3's `ALLOWED_SUFFIXES` contained the bare fragment `'Permission.php'`, tested with `str_contains()` against the whole path — which exempts **every** production file whose name ends that way, wherever it lives. The tree already has two: `dev:apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php` (the `require.any.permission:` middleware itself, `:12-19`) and `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/SupportAccess/Domain/Entities/ImpersonationSessionPermission.php` (`:10-21`). Neither carries a literal today, so nothing is currently mis-baselined — but a literal added to either **tomorrow** would pass silently, which is exactly the *"the door closes behind the existing sites"* guarantee failing. Rev 4 splits the list in two: **two anchored regexes** matching only the registry's own generated artefacts — `app/Modules/<M>/Domain/Authorization/<M>PermissionManifest.php` and `app/Modules/<M>/Domain/Enums/<M>Permission.php` — and a list of **prefixes anchored at the project root**, matched with `str_starts_with()` against the normalised relative path from B3-4 rather than with `str_contains()` against an absolute one. `tests/` stays a prefix, and `'/tests/'`-as-substring is gone with it. The fixture set gains a `RequireAnyPermission`-shaped file proving it is **not** exempt.

**The rule, in full** (plan gate r2, **B2-5**; rev 2 supplied the registration, the message table and the injected-key-file design, and no class body):

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\PHPStan\Rules;

use PhpParser\Node;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr\CallLike;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr\MethodCall;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr\StaticCall;
use PHPStan\Analyser\Scope;
use PHPStan\Rules\Rule;
use PHPStan\Rules\RuleErrorBuilder;

/**
 * Two error classes, and the SECOND is the important one.
 *
 * Flagging only literals ALREADY IN the registry lets the single most damaging
 * mistake through: `invoices.posst` is in no manifest, and at runtime Spatie
 * swallows PermissionDoesNotExist, so the route silently 403s for EVERYONE
 * including admin.
 *
 * The shape match is restricted to authorization CALL SITES rather than to any
 * dotted string anywhere, which is what keeps the rule free of false positives
 * on config keys and i18n keys.
 *
 * It reads the registry from a GENERATED KEY FILE and never boots Laravel: a
 * rule that called app() inside processNode() would boot the framework per
 * analysed file. The file is written by `permissions:export-frontend-map` and
 * is committed in the SAME commit as this rule (plan gate r2, B2-4).
 *
 * @implements Rule<CallLike>
 */
final class ForbidPermissionStringLiteral implements Rule
{
    /** Method calls whose string arguments are permission keys. */
    private const PERMISSION_METHODS = ['can', 'cant', 'hasPermissionTo', 'hasAnyPermission', 'hasAllPermissions'];

    /** Gate::* statics whose FIRST string argument is a permission key. */
    private const GATE_METHODS = ['authorize', 'allows', 'denies'];

    /** Middleware strings carry the key after a prefix. */
    private const MIDDLEWARE_PREFIXES = ['can:', 'require.any.permission:'];

    /** The catalogue's frozen key shape (spec 4.2.1), used to decide whether a literal is permission-shaped at all. */
    private const KEY_PATTERN = '/^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_.-]*)+$/';

    /**
     * The registry's OWN generated artefacts, matched as anchored paths relative
     * to the analysed project root (plan gate r3, M3-2).
     *
     * Rev 3 had the bare suffix fragment 'Permission.php' here, checked with
     * str_contains(), which exempted every production file ending that way —
     * app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php and
     * app/Modules/SupportAccess/Domain/Entities/ImpersonationSessionPermission.php
     * among them. A literal added to either would have bypassed the guard.
     *
     * @var list<non-empty-string>
     */
    private const ALLOWED_PATH_PATTERNS = [
        '#^app/Modules/[A-Za-z0-9]+/Domain/Authorization/[A-Za-z0-9]+PermissionManifest\.php$#',
        '#^app/Modules/[A-Za-z0-9]+/Domain/Enums/[A-Za-z0-9]+Permission\.php$#',
    ];

    /**
     * Paths whose dotted literals legitimately name the catalogue. PREFIXES,
     * anchored at the project root — never substrings of an absolute path.
     *
     * @var list<non-empty-string>
     */
    private const ALLOWED_PATH_PREFIXES = [
        'app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/',
        'database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php',
        'app/Console/Commands/SyncPermissions.php',
        'app/Console/Commands/ScaffoldPermission.php',
        'app/Console/Commands/ReportDeadPermissions.php',
        'tests/',
    ];

    /** @var list<string>|null */
    private ?array $keys = null;

    /** @var list<string>|null */
    private ?array $baseline = null;

    private bool $keyFileErrorReported = false;

    /**
     * @param  string  $projectRoot  the analysed project root, injected from
     *                               phpstan.neon's %currentWorkingDirectory% —
     *                               the SAME getcwd() tools/generate-permission-baselines.php
     *                               strips, because both run from apps/api. A
     *                               rule cannot discover it: PHPStan absolutizes
     *                               analysed paths (phpstan.phar
     *                               src/Command/CommandHelper.php:194) and
     *                               Scope::getFile() returns them absolute, so
     *                               without this the generated baseline's
     *                               relative sites can never match (plan gate r3,
     *                               BLOCKER B3-4).
     */
    public function __construct(
        private readonly string $projectRoot,
        private readonly string $keyFile,
        private readonly string $baselineFile,
    ) {}

    public function getNodeType(): string
    {
        return CallLike::class;
    }

    /**
     * @param  CallLike  $node
     * @return list<\PHPStan\Rules\IdentifierRuleError>
     */
    public function processNode(Node $node, Scope $scope): array
    {
        // The SAME normalisation the baseline generator performs, so a generated
        // site and a runtime site are the same string (plan gate r3, B3-4).
        $file = $this->relativePath($scope->getFile());

        if ($this->keys === null) {
            if (! is_file($this->keyFile)) {
                if ($this->keyFileErrorReported) {
                    return [];
                }
                $this->keyFileErrorReported = true;

                // ONE explicit error, never a silent pass over every literal.
                return [
                    RuleErrorBuilder::message(
                        'permission-registry-keys.json is missing; run `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map`. '
                        .'Without it this rule cannot tell a real key from a typo, and a typo silently 403s every caller including admin.',
                    )->identifier('permission.registryKeyFileMissing')->build(),
                ];
            }
            /** @var list<string> $decoded */
            $decoded = json_decode((string) file_get_contents($this->keyFile), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);
            $this->keys = $decoded;
            /** @var list<string> $baseline */
            $baseline = is_file($this->baselineFile)
                ? json_decode((string) file_get_contents($this->baselineFile), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR)
                : [];
            $this->baseline = $baseline;
        }

        if ($this->isAllowedPath($file)) {
            return [];
        }

        $errors = [];
        foreach ($this->literalsAt($node) as $literal) {
            foreach ($this->keysIn($literal) as $key) {
                if (preg_match(self::KEY_PATTERN, $key) !== 1) {
                    continue;
                }
                $site = $file.':'.$node->getStartLine().':'.$key;
                if (in_array($site, $this->baseline ?? [], true)) {
                    continue;   // shrink-only: the door closes BEHIND the existing sites
                }

                $errors[] = in_array($key, $this->keys ?? [], true)
                    ? RuleErrorBuilder::message(sprintf(
                        "permission literal '%s' — use the module enum case's ->value instead of the string.",
                        $key,
                    ))->identifier('permission.literal')->build()
                    : RuleErrorBuilder::message(sprintf(
                        "unknown permission literal '%s' — it is in no PermissionManifest, so this gate denies every caller "
                        .'including admin. Declare it, or fix the typo.',
                        $key,
                    ))->identifier('permission.unknownLiteral')->build();
            }
        }

        return $errors;
    }

    /**
     * The analysed file's path RELATIVE to the project root, with forward
     * slashes and no leading separator — byte-identical to what
     * tools/generate-permission-baselines.php writes for the same file, which is
     * the whole point (plan gate r3, B3-4). A file outside the root (there
     * should be none, since PHPStan analyses `app/`) keeps its absolute path
     * rather than being silently half-stripped.
     */
    private function relativePath(string $file): string
    {
        $normalised = str_replace('\\', '/', $file);
        $root = rtrim(str_replace('\\', '/', $this->projectRoot), '/').'/';

        return str_starts_with($normalised, $root)
            ? substr($normalised, strlen($root))
            : $normalised;
    }

    /** The registry's own manifests/enums, plus the root-anchored allow-list (plan gate r3, M3-2). */
    private function isAllowedPath(string $relative): bool
    {
        foreach (self::ALLOWED_PATH_PATTERNS as $pattern) {
            if (preg_match($pattern, $relative) === 1) {
                return true;
            }
        }

        foreach (self::ALLOWED_PATH_PREFIXES as $prefix) {
            if (str_starts_with($relative, $prefix)) {
                return true;
            }
        }

        return false;
    }

    /**
     * The string literals this call site treats as permission keys. Anything
     * that is not a plain String_ — an enum ->value, a concat, a variable — is
     * NOT a literal and is deliberately invisible to this rule.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    private function literalsAt(CallLike $node): array
    {
        $name = null;
        if ($node instanceof MethodCall && $node->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
            $name = $node->name->toString();
        } elseif ($node instanceof StaticCall && $node->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
            $name = $node->name->toString();
            if (! ($node->class instanceof Node\Name) || $node->class->getLast() !== 'Gate') {
                return [];
            }
            if (! in_array($name, self::GATE_METHODS, true)) {
                return [];
            }
        }

        if ($name === null) {
            return [];
        }
        if ($node instanceof MethodCall
            && ! in_array($name, self::PERMISSION_METHODS, true)
            && $name !== 'middleware') {
            return [];
        }

        $literals = [];
        foreach ($node->getArgs() as $argument) {
            foreach ($this->flatten($argument->value) as $string) {
                $literals[] = $string;
            }
        }

        return $literals;
    }

    /**
     * A String_, or every String_ element of an array literal. One level is
     * enough: `->middleware(['can:a', 'can:b'])` is the only nested shape.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    private function flatten(Node\Expr $expr): array
    {
        if ($expr instanceof Node\Scalar\String_) {
            return [$expr->value];
        }

        if ($expr instanceof Node\Expr\Array_) {
            $out = [];
            foreach ($expr->items as $item) {
                if ($item !== null && $item->value instanceof Node\Scalar\String_) {
                    $out[] = $item->value->value;
                }
            }

            return $out;
        }

        return [];
    }

    /**
     * A middleware string carries its keys after a prefix and may list several,
     * comma-separated. Everything else IS the key.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    private function keysIn(string $literal): array
    {
        foreach (self::MIDDLEWARE_PREFIXES as $prefix) {
            if (str_starts_with($literal, $prefix)) {
                return array_values(array_filter(array_map(trim(...), explode(',', substr($literal, strlen($prefix))))));
            }
        }

        return [$literal];
    }
}
~~~

**The baseline is a list of `<file>:<line>:<key>` triples, generated, never hand-written** — a site, not a message, so re-wording the message never invalidates it and moving a call site never silently re-baselines it.

**The ignore list is large, and that is the honest cost. The baseline is GENERATED by a committed script, never by a `php -r '…extract…'` ellipsis** (plan gate r2, MAJOR **M2-6**). Rev 2's generator was literally `php -r '…extract this rule identifier'"'"'s messages, sort, write permission-literal-baseline.json…'`, which is not a command. It is now a small committed script, `apps/api/tools/generate-permission-baselines.php`, run twice — once per rule — and staged with the baselines it writes:

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

/**
 * Generate a shrink-only PHPStan baseline for one of wave 1's two permission
 * rules, as a sorted list of `<file>:<line>:<key>` (or `<file>:<line>`) sites.
 *
 * Usage: php tools/generate-permission-baselines.php <identifier> <out-path>
 *   php tools/generate-permission-baselines.php permission.literal              tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json
 *   php tools/generate-permission-baselines.php permission.roleNameAuthorization tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json
 *
 * It reads PHPStan's JSON error format on STDIN, so the caller controls the
 * analysis and this script does exactly one thing.
 *
 * SITE PATHS ARE RELATIVE TO getcwd(), and BOTH rules strip the SAME directory
 * through their injected `projectRoot` (phpstan.neon's %currentWorkingDirectory%).
 * PHPStan absolutizes analysed paths before analysis (phpstan.phar
 * src/Command/CommandHelper.php:194) and Scope::getFile() returns them absolute,
 * so if this script and the rules ever normalise differently the baseline
 * absorbs NOTHING and the whole-app run is red (plan gate r3, BLOCKER B3-4).
 * RUN THIS FROM apps/api, which is where every command block in Task 1.16 cds to.
 */
$identifier = $argv[1] ?? null;
$out = $argv[2] ?? null;
if (! is_string($identifier) || ! is_string($out)) {
    fwrite(STDERR, "usage: generate-permission-baselines.php <identifier> <out-path>\n");
    exit(1);
}

/** @var array{files?: array<string, array{messages: list<array{message: string, line: int|null, identifier?: string}>}>} $report */
$report = json_decode((string) stream_get_contents(STDIN), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);

$sites = [];
foreach ($report['files'] ?? [] as $file => $entry) {
    $relative = str_replace(getcwd().'/', '', str_replace('\\', '/', $file));
    foreach ($entry['messages'] as $message) {
        if (($message['identifier'] ?? null) !== $identifier && ! str_starts_with($message['identifier'] ?? '', $identifier)) {
            continue;
        }
        $line = $message['line'] ?? 0;

        // The literal rules quote the key between the first pair of single
        // quotes; the role-name rule has no key, so the site is file:line.
        $site = preg_match("/'([^']+)'/", $message['message'], $m) === 1
            ? $relative.':'.$line.':'.$m[1]
            : $relative.':'.$line;

        $sites[] = $site;
    }
}

$sites = array_values(array_unique($sites));
sort($sites, SORT_STRING);

file_put_contents($out, json_encode($sites, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE)."\n");
fwrite(STDOUT, count($sites)." sites baselined into {$out}\n");
~~~

and the two invocations, in full:
```
cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 --error-format=json --no-progress app > /tmp/w1-phpstan.json
php tools/generate-permission-baselines.php permission.literal \
    tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json < /tmp/w1-phpstan.json
php tools/generate-permission-baselines.php permission.roleNameAuthorization \
    tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json < /tmp/w1-phpstan.json
```
> **`permission.literal` matches both literal identifiers** (`permission.literal` and `permission.unknownLiteral`) through the `str_starts_with` clause — deliberately, because a pre-existing **typo** must be baselined too or the wave cannot land, and R-w1-3 records that wave 3 empties the list.

Record the two counts the second command prints **as measured**, not as rev 1's remembered `608 / 309`. What the baseline buys is that the **door closes behind** the existing sites: a *new* literal is an error on day one, and the list only shrinks (wave 3 empties it).

### 16b — `RoutePermissionLiteralTest` (AST, with constant-expression evaluation)

**PHPStan analyses `app/` only** (`dev:apps/api/phpstan.neon:5-8`), so the rule **does** reach module route files under `app/Modules/*/Presentation/` — it is only the **top-level `routes/` directory** that is outside its paths. Rev 1's *"PHPStan cannot see route files"* was overbroad; the sentence after it had the distinction right (plan gate r1, minor). Widening PHPStan's paths would pull unrelated files into level 8 and need its own baseline, so a companion architecture test parses **every `app/Modules/*/Presentation/routes*.php` and every file under `routes/`** with `nikic/php-parser` (already a PHPStan dependency, vendored at `apps/api/vendor/nikic/php-parser`), extracts each `can:` / `require.any.permission:` argument, and asserts every extracted key is in `activeKeys()`.

**It STATICALLY EVALUATES constant expressions and ACCEPTS them.** A guard that rejects the migration target is not a guard, it is a wall — and `'can:'.InventoryPermission::TransfersReconcile->value` is exactly the form this design prescribes:

| Expression in a route file | Handling |
|---|---|
| `'can:inventory.transfers.reconcile'` | plain `String_` — resolved, reported as a **literal** (use the enum) **and** checked against the registry |
| `'can:'.InventoryPermission::TransfersReconcile->value` | `Concat` of a `String_` and an enum-case `->value` — **evaluated and ACCEPTED**; the resolved key is checked against the registry |
| an **array literal** of either of the above inside `->middleware([...])` — `->middleware(['can:'.A::X->value, 'can:'.B::Y->value])` | **evaluated and ACCEPTED, element by element.** `middlewareExpressions()` walks one level of array literal and yields each element as its own expression with its own line, so each is resolved or reported independently |
| `implode(',', [Enum::A->value, Enum::B->value])` or any other `FuncCall` | **NOT accepted — reported as unresolvable.** The evaluator's fallback handles `Enum::Case->value` only and has no `FuncCall` branch. There are **zero** such route gates in the tree today, so rev 4 states the limit rather than implementing a shape nothing uses (plan gate r3, minor 1: rev 3's table claimed this was accepted while its third "accepted shape" test case was in fact a second `Concat`). The first case's green run over the real tree is the evidence the limit costs nothing |

Resolution uses `nikic/php-parser`'s `ConstExprEvaluator` with an **enum-case fallback that reads the case's declared value from the module enum's own AST** — no autoloading, no reflection on a booted app, so the test stays a pure static walk.

**The test, in full** (plan gate r2, **B2-5**; rev 2 supplied a twelve-line evaluator fragment and prose):

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Architecture;

use PhpParser\ConstExprEvaluationException;
use PhpParser\ConstExprEvaluator;
use PhpParser\Node;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr;
use PhpParser\NodeFinder;
use PhpParser\ParserFactory;
use PHPUnit\Framework\TestCase;
use Symfony\Component\Finder\Finder;

/**
 * Every `can:` / `require.any.permission:` key in every route file is either a
 * CONSTANT EXPRESSION this test can resolve — and then checked against the
 * registry — or a failure.
 *
 * PHPStan analyses `app/` (dev:apps/api/phpstan.neon:5-8), so
 * ForbidPermissionStringLiteral already reaches module route files under
 * `app/Modules/<M>/Presentation/`. What it does NOT reach is the top-level
 * `routes/` directory, and widening PHPStan's paths would pull unrelated files
 * into level 8 and need its own baseline. This companion walk covers both, with
 * nikic/php-parser, which is already a PHPStan dependency.
 */
final class RoutePermissionLiteralTest extends TestCase
{
    /** The route files: every module's own, plus the top-level routes/ directory PHPStan does not analyse. */
    private const ROUTE_GLOBS = ['app/Modules', 'routes'];

    private const MIDDLEWARE_PREFIXES = ['can:', 'require.any.permission:'];

    /** @var array<string, array<string, string>>|null  short enum name => case name => value */
    private static ?array $enumIndex = null;

    /**
     * A guard that REJECTED the migration target would be a wall, not a guard:
     * `'can:'.InventoryPermission::TransfersReconcile->value` is the form this
     * whole design prescribes, so it is ACCEPTED and its resolved key checked.
     */
    public function test_every_route_permission_key_is_statically_resolvable_and_in_the_registry(): void
    {
        $keys = json_decode(
            (string) file_get_contents(__DIR__.'/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json'),
            true,
            512,
            JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
        );
        self::assertIsArray($keys);

        $unresolvable = [];
        $unknown = [];

        foreach ($this->routeFiles() as $file) {
            foreach ($this->middlewareExpressions($file) as [$expr, $line]) {
                try {
                    $value = $this->evaluator()->evaluateSilently($expr);
                } catch (ConstExprEvaluationException) {
                    $unresolvable[] = sprintf(
                        'route permission key at %s:%d is not statically resolvable; a route gate must be a constant '
                        .'expression so CI can verify it exists.',
                        $file,
                        $line,
                    );

                    continue;
                }

                if (! is_string($value)) {
                    continue;   // a middleware alias with no permission payload
                }

                foreach ($this->keysIn($value) as $key) {
                    if (! in_array($key, $keys, true)) {
                        $unknown[] = sprintf("%s:%d declares '%s', which is in no PermissionManifest.", $file, $line, $key);
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        self::assertSame([], $unresolvable, implode("\n", $unresolvable));
        self::assertSame([], $unknown, implode("\n", $unknown));
    }

    /**
     * The three ACCEPTED shapes, one case each, over a fixture rather than the
     * real tree: a plain String_, a Concat with an enum-case ->value, and either
     * of those as an ELEMENT of a ->middleware([...]) array literal — which
     * middlewareExpressions() yields as its own expression, so it is resolved by
     * the same evaluator on its own (plan gate r3, minor 1).
     */
    public function test_the_three_constant_shapes_are_accepted(): void
    {
        $plain = new Node\Scalar\String_('can:inventory.transfers.reconcile');
        self::assertSame('can:inventory.transfers.reconcile', $this->evaluator()->evaluateSilently($plain));

        $concat = new Expr\BinaryOp\Concat(
            new Node\Scalar\String_('can:'),
            new Expr\PropertyFetch(
                new Expr\ClassConstFetch(new Node\Name('InventoryPermission'), new Node\Identifier('TransfersReconcile')),
                new Node\Identifier('value'),
            ),
        );
        self::assertSame('can:inventory.transfers.reconcile', $this->evaluator()->evaluateSilently($concat));

        // The third shape: the SAME two expressions as elements of one array
        // literal. The assertion is over the extractor AND the evaluator
        // together, which is what makes it a distinct shape rather than a second
        // copy of the case above.
        $secondConcat = new Expr\BinaryOp\Concat(
            new Node\Scalar\String_('can:'),
            new Expr\PropertyFetch(
                new Expr\ClassConstFetch(new Node\Name('InventoryPermission'), new Node\Identifier('TransfersClose')),
                new Node\Identifier('value'),
            ),
        );
        $array = new Expr\Array_([
            new Node\ArrayItem($concat),
            new Node\ArrayItem($secondConcat),
        ]);

        $resolved = [];
        foreach ($array->items as $item) {
            self::assertNotNull($item);
            $resolved[] = $this->evaluator()->evaluateSilently($item->value);
        }
        self::assertSame(['can:inventory.transfers.reconcile', 'can:inventory.transfers.close'], $resolved);
    }

    /** The rejected shapes. There are zero such sites in the tree today, which the first case proves. */
    public function test_a_non_constant_expression_is_rejected(): void
    {
        $this->expectException(ConstExprEvaluationException::class);

        $this->evaluator()->evaluateSilently(
            new Expr\BinaryOp\Concat(new Node\Scalar\String_('can:'), new Expr\Variable('key')),
        );
    }

    /**
     * A FuncCall is NOT a shape this evaluator accepts, and the plan says so
     * rather than claiming otherwise (plan gate r3, minor 1). If a route ever
     * needs `implode(',', [...])`, add the branch AND this case flips.
     */
    public function test_a_function_call_is_rejected(): void
    {
        $this->expectException(ConstExprEvaluationException::class);

        $this->evaluator()->evaluateSilently(
            new Expr\FuncCall(new Node\Name('implode'), [
                new Node\Arg(new Node\Scalar\String_(',')),
                new Node\Arg(new Expr\Array_([])),
            ]),
        );
    }

    private function evaluator(): ConstExprEvaluator
    {
        return new ConstExprEvaluator(function (Expr $expr): string {
            // PropertyFetch on a ClassConstFetch: Enum::Case->value
            if ($expr instanceof Expr\PropertyFetch
                && $expr->name instanceof Node\Identifier && $expr->name->toString() === 'value'
                && $expr->var instanceof Expr\ClassConstFetch
                && $expr->var->class instanceof Node\Name
                && $expr->var->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
                return $this->enumCaseValueFromAst($expr->var->class->toString(), $expr->var->name->toString());
            }

            throw new ConstExprEvaluationException('not statically resolvable');
        });
    }

    /**
     * Map a (possibly namespaced) enum class name to its declared case values
     * through a one-pass index over every module enum, built once per test run.
     * No autoloading, no reflection on a booted app.
     */
    private function enumCaseValueFromAst(string $class, string $case): string
    {
        self::$enumIndex ??= $this->buildEnumIndex();

        $position = strrpos($class, '\\');
        $short = $position === false ? $class : substr($class, $position + 1);

        $value = self::$enumIndex[$short][$case] ?? null;
        if ($value === null) {
            throw new ConstExprEvaluationException(sprintf('%s::%s has no statically readable value.', $short, $case));
        }

        return $value;
    }

    /** @return array<string, array<string, string>> */
    private function buildEnumIndex(): array
    {
        $parser = (new ParserFactory)->createForHostVersion();
        $finder = new NodeFinder;

        $index = [];
        foreach ((new Finder)->files()->in(base_path('app/Modules'))->path('Domain/Enums')->name('*Permission.php') as $file) {
            $ast = $parser->parse($file->getContents()) ?? [];
            /** @var list<Node\Stmt\Enum_> $enums */
            $enums = $finder->findInstanceOf($ast, Node\Stmt\Enum_::class);
            foreach ($enums as $enum) {
                $name = $enum->name?->toString();
                if ($name === null) {
                    continue;
                }
                foreach ($enum->stmts as $statement) {
                    if ($statement instanceof Node\Stmt\EnumCase && $statement->expr instanceof Node\Scalar\String_) {
                        $index[$name][$statement->name->toString()] = $statement->expr->value;
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        return $index;
    }

    /** @return list<string> */
    private function routeFiles(): array
    {
        $files = [];
        foreach ((new Finder)->files()->in(base_path(self::ROUTE_GLOBS[0]))->name('routes*.php') as $file) {
            $files[] = $file->getPathname();
        }
        foreach ((new Finder)->files()->in(base_path(self::ROUTE_GLOBS[1]))->name('*.php') as $file) {
            $files[] = $file->getPathname();
        }
        sort($files);

        return $files;
    }

    /**
     * Every argument of every `->middleware(...)` call in one file, one level of
     * array literal included. An AST WALK, not a grep: nothing is silently
     * skipped, because every extracted argument is either resolved or reported.
     *
     * @return list<array{0: Expr, 1: int}>
     */
    private function middlewareExpressions(string $file): array
    {
        $parser = (new ParserFactory)->createForHostVersion();
        $ast = $parser->parse((string) file_get_contents($file)) ?? [];

        /** @var list<Expr\MethodCall|Expr\StaticCall> $calls */
        $calls = (new NodeFinder)->find($ast, static fn (Node $node): bool => ($node instanceof Expr\MethodCall || $node instanceof Expr\StaticCall)
            && $node->name instanceof Node\Identifier
            && $node->name->toString() === 'middleware');

        $out = [];
        foreach ($calls as $call) {
            foreach ($call->getArgs() as $argument) {
                if ($argument->value instanceof Expr\Array_) {
                    foreach ($argument->value->items as $item) {
                        if ($item !== null) {
                            $out[] = [$item->value, $item->getStartLine()];
                        }
                    }

                    continue;
                }
                $out[] = [$argument->value, $argument->getStartLine()];
            }
        }

        return $out;
    }

    /** @return list<string> */
    private function keysIn(string $literal): array
    {
        foreach (self::MIDDLEWARE_PREFIXES as $prefix) {
            if (str_starts_with($literal, $prefix)) {
                return array_values(array_filter(array_map(trim(...), explode(',', substr($literal, strlen($prefix))))));
            }
        }

        return [];
    }
}
~~~

> **It reads the same `permission-registry-keys.json` the PHPStan rule reads**, which is the second reason that fixture must land in **this** commit (plan gate r2, B2-4): without it the test's first case cannot run at all.

### 16c — `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization`

The eight live role-name authorization sites — `CreateDraftCountingRequest.php:35`, `InventoryCountingController.php:782,866,905,975,1297,1494`, `DiscountPermissionResolver.php:39` — **bypass token narrowing** and are a **wave 2a** conversion and a **wave 2b entry condition**. Wave 1 ships the **rule and its shrink-only baseline** so no ninth site can be added; it does **not** convert the eight. The rule matches `->hasRole(`, `->hasAnyRole(`, `->roles->contains(`, and `in_array($…->name, [ … ], true)` where the array elements are known role names.

> Spec §8's wave-1 deliverable list includes this rule and its ESLint companion. It is included here for that reason; the wave-1 brief's shorter list is not a scope reduction.

**The rule, in full** (plan gate r2, **B2-5**):

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\PHPStan\Rules;

use PhpParser\Node;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr\CallLike;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr\FuncCall;
use PhpParser\Node\Expr\MethodCall;
use PHPStan\Analyser\Scope;
use PHPStan\Rules\Rule;
use PHPStan\Rules\RuleErrorBuilder;

/**
 * Role-name authorization BYPASSES TOKEN NARROWING: a scoped token restricts
 * permissions, not role membership, so `$user->hasRole('manager')` is true for a
 * token that was issued without a single one of manager's permissions.
 *
 * Wave 1 ships the rule and a shrink-only baseline so no NINTH site can be
 * added. It converts none of the eight — that is wave 2a, and a wave-2b entry
 * condition (R-w1-1).
 *
 * @implements Rule<CallLike>
 */
final class ForbidRoleNameAuthorization implements Rule
{
    private const ROLE_METHODS = ['hasRole', 'hasAnyRole', 'hasAllRoles', 'hasExactRoles'];

    /**
     * The eight SystemRoleTemplate values, duplicated here BY NECESSITY: a
     * PHPStan rule runs in its own process and must not boot Laravel to read an
     * enum. SystemRoleTemplateAlignmentTest asserts this list equals the enum,
     * so the duplication cannot drift silently.
     *
     * @var list<non-empty-string>
     */
    private const ROLE_NAMES = ['admin', 'general_manager', 'manager', 'cashier', 'viewer', 'technician', 'operator', 'accountant'];

    /** @var list<string>|null */
    private ?array $baseline = null;

    /**
     * @param  string  $projectRoot  see ForbidPermissionStringLiteral — the two
     *                               rules normalise site paths IDENTICALLY, or
     *                               their generated baselines absorb nothing
     *                               (plan gate r3, BLOCKER B3-4).
     */
    public function __construct(
        private readonly string $projectRoot,
        private readonly string $baselineFile,
    ) {}

    public function getNodeType(): string
    {
        return CallLike::class;
    }

    /**
     * @param  CallLike  $node
     * @return list<\PHPStan\Rules\IdentifierRuleError>
     */
    public function processNode(Node $node, Scope $scope): array
    {
        $file = $this->relativePath($scope->getFile());
        if (str_starts_with($file, 'tests/')) {
            return [];
        }

        if ($this->baseline === null) {
            /** @var list<string> $baseline */
            $baseline = is_file($this->baselineFile)
                ? json_decode((string) file_get_contents($this->baselineFile), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR)
                : [];
            $this->baseline = $baseline;
        }

        $idiom = $this->idiomAt($node);
        if ($idiom === null) {
            return [];
        }

        $site = $file.':'.$node->getStartLine();
        if (in_array($site, $this->baseline, true)) {
            return [];   // shrink-only
        }

        return [
            RuleErrorBuilder::message(sprintf(
                'role-name authorization (%s) bypasses token narrowing: a scoped token restricts PERMISSIONS, not role '
                .'membership. Gate on the permission the role happens to hold instead.',
                $idiom,
            ))->identifier('permission.roleNameAuthorization')->build(),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Byte-identical to ForbidPermissionStringLiteral::relativePath() and to
     * what tools/generate-permission-baselines.php writes. Duplicated rather
     * than shared because a PHPStan rule has no container to inject a collaborator
     * from, and a two-line trait is more indirection than it removes; the two
     * rules' fixture tests both load a GENERATED baseline entry, so a drift
     * between them is red rather than silent (plan gate r3, B3-4).
     */
    private function relativePath(string $file): string
    {
        $normalised = str_replace('\\', '/', $file);
        $root = rtrim(str_replace('\\', '/', $this->projectRoot), '/').'/';

        return str_starts_with($normalised, $root)
            ? substr($normalised, strlen($root))
            : $normalised;
    }

    /** The idiom's name, or null when this call is not one. */
    private function idiomAt(CallLike $node): ?string
    {
        if ($node instanceof MethodCall && $node->name instanceof Node\Identifier) {
            $name = $node->name->toString();

            if (in_array($name, self::ROLE_METHODS, true)) {
                return '->'.$name.'()';
            }

            // ->roles->contains('manager') / ->roles->contains('name', 'manager')
            if ($name === 'contains'
                && $node->var instanceof Node\Expr\PropertyFetch
                && $node->var->name instanceof Node\Identifier
                && $node->var->name->toString() === 'roles'
                && $this->mentionsARoleName($node)) {
                return '->roles->contains()';
            }

            return null;
        }

        // in_array($user->role->name, ['manager', 'admin'], true)
        if ($node instanceof FuncCall
            && $node->name instanceof Node\Name
            && $node->name->toString() === 'in_array'
            && $this->mentionsARoleName($node)) {
            return 'in_array() over role names';
        }

        return null;
    }

    /** True when any string argument, at any one nesting level, is a known role name. */
    private function mentionsARoleName(CallLike $node): bool
    {
        foreach ($node->getArgs() as $argument) {
            $value = $argument->value;

            if ($value instanceof Node\Scalar\String_ && in_array($value->value, self::ROLE_NAMES, true)) {
                return true;
            }

            if ($value instanceof Node\Expr\Array_) {
                foreach ($value->items as $item) {
                    if ($item !== null
                        && $item->value instanceof Node\Scalar\String_
                        && in_array($item->value->value, self::ROLE_NAMES, true)) {
                        return true;
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        return false;
    }
}
~~~

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 0 — generate and stage `permission-registry-keys.json` FIRST, in this commit** (plan gate r2, **B2-4**). The tinker line is above; expected **327**. Nothing else in this task can be green before it exists: the PHPStan rule errors on its absence by design, and `RoutePermissionLiteralTest` reads it too.
- [ ] **Step 1** — write both PHPStan rules (bodies above), and register them in `apps/api/phpstan.neon`. **They go in different sections and that is not a detail** (plan gate r2, minor): `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` takes two constructor arguments, so it belongs under **`services:`** beside `ForbidFloatCastOnDecimalProperty` (`dev:apps/api/phpstan.neon:43-47`); `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` takes one, so it goes under **`services:`** too — the `rules:` list at `dev:…:33-41` accepts only argument-free rules. The File Structure table says the same.
      ```neon
      services:
          -
              class: App\PHPStan\Rules\ForbidPermissionStringLiteral
              arguments:
                  projectRoot: %currentWorkingDirectory%
                  keyFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json
                  baselineFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json
              tags:
                  - phpstan.rules.rule
          -
              class: App\PHPStan\Rules\ForbidRoleNameAuthorization
              arguments:
                  projectRoot: %currentWorkingDirectory%
                  baselineFile: %currentWorkingDirectory%/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json
              tags:
                  - phpstan.rules.rule
      ```
      **`projectRoot: %currentWorkingDirectory%` is load-bearing, not boilerplate** (plan gate r3, **B3-4**). It is how each rule learns the analysed root, and it must be **the same directory the baseline generator runs from** — `apps/api` in every command block in this task — because the generator strips `getcwd().'/'` while PHPStan hands `Scope::getFile()` an **absolute** path (`phpstan.phar` `src/Command/CommandHelper.php:194`). Both rules normalise with the identical `relativePath()` helper; if the two ever disagree, **no** baselined site is absorbed and Step 6's whole-`app/` run is red on every pre-existing literal.
- [ ] **Step 2 — generate both shrink-only baselines** with `tools/generate-permission-baselines.php` and the two invocations above, and record the measured counts. **The baselines must be generated BEFORE the whole-app run in Step 6**, or that run reports every pre-existing site as an error.
- [ ] **Step 3 — fixture pairs under `apps/api/tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/`** plus `ForbidPermissionStringLiteralTest` (the shape of `ForbidFixedScaleQuantityLiteralRuleTest`), proving the rule fires on **both** error classes and does **not** over-fire on config keys, i18n keys or a dotted string in a non-authorization argument. The same fixture pattern covers `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization`. **Three cases rev 4 adds, and each is a named gate finding:**
      - **`a_generated_baseline_entry_absorbs_its_own_site`** (**B3-4**) — the round trip, end to end, with **no hand-written path anywhere in the test**. Run the rule over a fixture with a known literal and capture the error; feed a one-message PHPStan JSON report through `tools/generate-permission-baselines.php` (or construct the site with the *generator's own* `getcwd()`-stripping expression, never with a string the test author typed); re-run the rule with that file as `baselineFile` and assert **zero** errors. **Against rev 3's rules this is red**: the baseline holds `tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/…`, the rule tests `/…/apps/api/tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/…`, and nothing matches. Paste the red into the handback — it is the only mechanical proof B3-4 is closed;
      - **`a_relative_baseline_never_matches_an_unnormalised_absolute_site`** — the same fixture with `projectRoot: ''`, asserting the error **does** fire. This pins *why* the parameter exists, so a later refactor that drops it fails here rather than silently re-opening 600 sites;
      - **`a_production_file_merely_ending_in_Permission_php_is_not_exempt`** (**M3-2**) — a fixture at a `Fixtures/RequireAnyPermission.php`-shaped path (and a second under a `Domain/Entities/` segment, mirroring `ImpersonationSessionPermission.php`) carrying a `->can('invoices.post')`; assert the rule **fires**. Then the paired positives: a fixture at `app/Modules/Inventory/Domain/Enums/InventoryPermission.php`'s shape and one at `app/Modules/Inventory/Domain/Authorization/InventoryPermissionManifest.php`'s shape are **exempt**. **Against rev 3's `str_contains($file, 'Permission.php')` the first two are green — wrongly — which is the named red.**
- [ ] **Step 4 — `RoutePermissionLiteralTest`** (body above): `test_the_three_constant_shapes_are_accepted`, `test_a_non_constant_expression_is_rejected`, **`test_a_function_call_is_rejected`** (plan gate r3, minor 1 — the evaluator has no `FuncCall` branch and rev 4 pins that rather than claiming otherwise), and `test_every_route_permission_key_is_statically_resolvable_and_in_the_registry` over the real tree. **The third is expected to be green on its first run**, and that is the evidence for the claim *"there are zero non-constant route gates in the tree today"* — paste it into the handback rather than asserting it.
      > **One of its accepted-shape cases is this wave's own new route.** Task 1.12 ships `->middleware('can:'.IdentityPermission::RolesManage->value)`, which is a `Concat` of a `String_` and an enum-case `->value` — so the wave's only new gate is also the guard's worked example (plan gate r2, M2-3).
- [ ] **Step 5 — the ESLint companion** for the role-name idiom in `apps/web` (`no-role-name-authorization`), with its own shrink-only allowlist in `apps/web/eslint.config.js`.
- [ ] **Step 6 — run PHPStan over the whole `app/` and confirm the baseline absorbs exactly the pre-existing sites and no more.** A baseline that absorbs a site introduced by *this wave* is a bug: check the diff.
      ```

      ./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app
      php artisan test tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionLiteralTest.php tests/PHPStan/ForbidPermissionStringLiteralTest.php tests/PHPStan/ForbidRoleNameAuthorizationTest.php
      cd ../web && pnpm lint
      ```

- [ ] **Step 7 — stage and commit:**
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/PHPStan/Rules/ForbidPermissionStringLiteral.php \
              apps/api/app/PHPStan/Rules/ForbidRoleNameAuthorization.php \
              apps/api/tools/generate-permission-baselines.php \
              apps/api/phpstan.neon \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionLiteralTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/PHPStan/ForbidPermissionStringLiteralTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/PHPStan/ForbidRoleNameAuthorizationTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/ForbidPermissionStringLiteralFixture.php \
              apps/api/tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/ForbidRoleNameAuthorizationFixture.php \
              apps/web/eslint.config.js \
              apps/web/tools/eslint-rules/no-role-name-authorization.mjs
      ```
      **`apps/api/tests/PHPStan/Fixtures/` is staged as its two named files, not as a directory** (plan gate r2, MAJOR M2-6): staging a directory adds whatever happens to be in it, which is how an unrelated scratch file reaches a reviewed commit.
      Subject: **`Phase 1.16: add ForbidPermissionStringLiteral, ForbidRoleNameAuthorization, the AST route-literal test and the generated registry key fixture`**, both trailers.

## Task 1.17: the generated frontend map and the label skeleton

**`permissions:export-frontend-map` keeps its signature, its output path, its sha256 header and the preflight drift guard.** W-LOT-A-1a's determinism gate asserts byte identity across two generations **and** the committed artifact, and **that gate must keep passing**. Only its **input** changes, from the seeder's two statics to `PermissionRegistry`.

**The full diff to `ExportFrontendPermissionsMap`** (plan gate r2, **B2-5**; rev 2 supplied a constructor and two call-site lines and left `render()`'s new third parameter, the `PERMISSION_MODULES` emission and the keys-fixture write as prose). Measured against `dev:apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ExportFrontendPermissionsMap.php`, whose `handle()` is `:24-43` and whose `render()` is `:49-91`:

~~~php
// (1) imports — the seeder import goes, the registry arrives
-use Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder;
+use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;

// (2) the constructor — dev:…:19-22
 public function __construct(
     private readonly Filesystem $files,
+    private readonly PermissionRegistry $registry,
 ) {
     parent::__construct();
 }

// (3) handle() — replacing dev:…:28-31
-        $contents = $this->render(
-            RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::permissionNames(),
-            RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::rolePermissionGrants(),
-        );
+        $contents = $this->render(
+            $this->registry->keys(),
+            $this->registry->templateGrants(),
+            $this->registry->byModule(),
+        );

// (4) handle() — after the successful put() at dev:…:34-38, write the PHPStan
//     key fixture from the SAME source, so the two can never disagree.
+        $keyFixture = base_path('tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json');
+        $keys = $this->registry->keys();
+        sort($keys, SORT_STRING);
+        $this->files->ensureDirectoryExists(dirname($keyFixture));
+        $this->files->put(

+            json_encode($keys, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE | JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR)."\n",
+        );
+        $this->info('Exported '.count($keys).' registry keys to '.$keyFixture.'.');
~~~

and `render()` itself, in full — the parameter list gains `$permissionModules`, the body gains one emitted constant, and **the hashed body is unchanged in shape** so the drift guard behaves:

~~~php
    /**
     * @param  list<string>                $permissionNames
     * @param  array<string, list<string>> $rolePermissionGrants
     * @param  array<string, list<string>> $permissionModules   module => keys, from PermissionRegistry::byModule()
     */
    private function render(array $permissionNames, array $rolePermissionGrants, array $permissionModules): string
    {
        /** @var array<string, list<string>> $permissionRoles */
        $permissionRoles = [];
        foreach ($permissionNames as $permissionName) {
            $permissionRoles[$permissionName] = [];
        }

        foreach ($rolePermissionGrants as $roleName => $grantedPermissions) {
            foreach ($grantedPermissions as $permissionName) {
                if (! array_key_exists($permissionName, $permissionRoles)) {
                    throw new LogicException(sprintf(
                        'Role "%s" grants unknown permission "%s".',
                        $roleName,
                        $permissionName,
                    ));
                }

                $permissionRoles[$permissionName][] = $roleName;
            }
        }

        ksort($permissionRoles, SORT_STRING);

        // INVERT byModule(): the map the frontend needs is key => module, and
        // inverting here rather than in TypeScript keeps the generated file a
        // pure lookup with no runtime work.
        /** @var array<string, string> $moduleOf */
        $moduleOf = [];
        foreach ($permissionModules as $module => $keys) {
            foreach ($keys as $key) {
                $moduleOf[$key] = $module;
            }
        }
        ksort($moduleOf, SORT_STRING);

        $lines = ['export const PERMISSIONS = {'];
        foreach ($permissionRoles as $permissionName => $roleNames) {
            sort($roleNames, SORT_STRING);
            $renderedRoles = implode(', ', array_map($this->quote(...), $roleNames));
            $lines[] = sprintf('  %s: [%s],', $this->quote($permissionName), $renderedRoles);
        }
        $lines[] = '} as const';
        $lines[] = '';

        // PERMISSION_MODULES is GROUPING DATA, never an authorization input.
        // The frontend reviewer is asked to confirm exactly that.
        $lines[] = 'export const PERMISSION_MODULES = {';
        foreach ($moduleOf as $permissionName => $module) {
            $lines[] = sprintf('  %s: %s,', $this->quote($permissionName), $this->quote($module));
        }
        $lines[] = '} as const';
        $lines[] = '';
        $lines[] = 'export type Permission = keyof typeof PERMISSIONS';
        $lines[] = 'export type PermissionModule = (typeof PERMISSION_MODULES)[Permission]';

        $body = implode("\n", $lines)."\n";
        $hashedBody = "\n".$body;

        return implode("\n", [
            '// This file is generated. Do not edit it by hand.',
            '// Source: apps/api/app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/PermissionRegistry.php',
            '// Source hash: sha256:'.hash('sha256', $hashedBody),
        ])."\n".$hashedBody;
    }
~~~

> The header's `// Source:` line moves from `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php` to the registry — an intentional, one-line change to a **header**, and **the sha256 is computed over `$hashedBody`, which starts below the header** (`dev:…:84,89`), so the drift guard is unaffected. **Verify that with the two-generation run rather than assuming it.**
>
> **The keys fixture is regenerated here, not created here** (plan gate r2, **B2-4**): Task 1.16 already committed it. This task's `git diff` on `tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-registry-keys.json` must therefore be **empty**, which is a free determinism check — the tinker line in Task 1.16 and the exporter here read the same `registry->keys()` and sort it the same way. A non-empty diff means one of the two sorts differently, and that is a finding.

**The label gap is real and large.** `apps/web/src/locales/{en,fr,ar}/common.json` carry 40 module keys and 35 action keys, identical in all three locales, against 327 permissions — so the UI silently degrades to the raw key because `translateModule`/`translateAction` pass the raw string as the i18next fallback. **Re-measure the two gap numbers against the 38 modules and the actual action set** rather than carrying rev 1's `119 / 125`, which were computed against 304 keys and the old prefix grouping; record the measured pair in the handback.

**`permissions:export-label-skeleton`, in full** (plan gate r2, **B2-5**). It shares `permissions:scaffold`'s encoding contract — `JSON_PRETTY_PRINT|JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES|JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE`, `ksort(…, SORT_STRING)`, one trailing newline — for the same reason: `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE` or the Arabic bundle is rewritten into `\uXXXX` escapes on every run and the diff is unreadable.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Console\Commands;

use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
use Illuminate\Console\Command;
use Illuminate\Filesystem\Filesystem;

/**
 * Write, per locale, a COMPLETE `permissions` label block: every module label
 * and every action label the registry needs, existing translations preserved
 * and missing ones stubbed with the raw token.
 *
 * The point is that a translator receives a FILE TO EDIT rather than a diff to
 * reconstruct. It writes to storage/app/permission-labels/<locale>.json and
 * NEVER to the locale bundles: a generated Arabic placeholder committed as a
 * translation is worse than a visible gap.
 */
final class ExportPermissionLabelSkeleton extends Command
{
    protected $signature = 'permissions:export-label-skeleton
        {--locale=* : Limit to these locales; defaults to en, fr and ar}';

    protected $description = 'Write a complete permissions label skeleton per locale for a translator to fill in.';

    private const LOCALES = ['en', 'fr', 'ar'];

    private const JSON_FLAGS = JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE | JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR;

    public function __construct(
        private readonly Filesystem $files,
        private readonly PermissionRegistry $registry,
    ) {
        parent::__construct();
    }

    public function handle(): int
    {
        /** @var list<string> $requested */
        $requested = array_values(array_map(strval(...), (array) $this->option('locale')));
        $locales = $requested !== [] ? $requested : self::LOCALES;

        $modules = array_keys($this->registry->byModule());
        sort($modules, SORT_STRING);

        $actions = [];
        foreach ($this->registry->definitions() as $definition) {
            $actions[$definition->action()] = true;
        }
        $actions = array_keys($actions);
        sort($actions, SORT_STRING);

        $outputDirectory = storage_path('app/permission-labels');
        $this->files->ensureDirectoryExists($outputDirectory);

        foreach ($locales as $locale) {
            $bundlePath = base_path(sprintf('../web/src/locales/%s/common.json', $locale));
            if (! $this->files->exists($bundlePath)) {
                $this->error('no locale bundle at '.$bundlePath);

                return self::FAILURE;
            }

            /** @var array<string, mixed> $bundle */
            $bundle = json_decode($this->files->get($bundlePath), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $existing */
            $existing = is_array($bundle['permissions'] ?? null) ? $bundle['permissions'] : [];
            /** @var array<string, string> $existingModules */
            $existingModules = is_array($existing['modules'] ?? null) ? $existing['modules'] : [];
            /** @var array<string, string> $existingActions */
            $existingActions = is_array($existing['actions'] ?? null) ? $existing['actions'] : [];

            $moduleBlock = [];
            $missingModules = 0;
            foreach ($modules as $module) {
                $moduleBlock[$module] = $existingModules[$module] ?? $module;
                $missingModules += isset($existingModules[$module]) ? 0 : 1;
            }

            $actionBlock = [];
            $missingActions = 0;
            foreach ($actions as $action) {
                $actionBlock[$action] = $existingActions[$action] ?? $action;
                $missingActions += isset($existingActions[$action]) ? 0 : 1;
            }

            ksort($moduleBlock, SORT_STRING);
            ksort($actionBlock, SORT_STRING);

            $skeleton = ['permissions' => ['actions' => $actionBlock, 'modules' => $moduleBlock]];
            $path = $outputDirectory.'/'.$locale.'.json';
            $this->files->put($path, json_encode($skeleton, self::JSON_FLAGS)."\n");

            $this->info(sprintf(
                '%s: %d modules (%d missing) + %d actions (%d missing) -> %s',
                $locale,
                count($moduleBlock),
                $missingModules,
                count($actionBlock),
                $missingActions,
                $path,
            ));
        }

        $this->warn('These are SKELETONS, not translations. Do not paste a placeholder into fr/ar and call the gap closed — record R-w1-2 instead.');

        return self::SUCCESS;
    }
}
~~~

**`PermissionRegistryLabelCoverageTest`, in full** — it is one of the four tests plan gate r2 named as the only oracle for its property:

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Feature\Authorization;

use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
use Tests\TestCase;

/**
 * For every ACTIVE key, `permissions.modules.<module>` and
 * `permissions.actions.<action>` must exist in EACH of en, fr and ar.
 *
 * A missing key is not cosmetic: translateModule/translateAction pass the raw
 * string to i18next as its own fallback, so the operator's Roles matrix renders
 * `inventory.transfers.reconcile` as a column header.
 *
 * The failure message points at `php artisan permissions:export-label-skeleton`,
 * which writes a COMPLETE file to edit rather than a diff to reconstruct.
 */
final class PermissionRegistryLabelCoverageTest extends TestCase
{
    private const LOCALES = ['en', 'fr', 'ar'];

    public function test_every_active_key_has_a_module_and_an_action_label_in_all_three_locales(): void
    {
        $registry = $this->app->make(PermissionRegistry::class);

        $modules = [];
        $actions = [];
        foreach ($registry->definitions() as $definition) {
            if ($definition->deprecated) {
                continue;   // a deprecated key leaves every template and every surface
            }
            $modules[$definition->module] = true;
            $actions[$definition->action()] = true;
        }

        $missing = [];
        foreach (self::LOCALES as $locale) {
            $bundle = $this->bundle($locale);
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $permissions */
            $permissions = is_array($bundle['permissions'] ?? null) ? $bundle['permissions'] : [];
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $localeModules */
            $localeModules = is_array($permissions['modules'] ?? null) ? $permissions['modules'] : [];
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $localeActions */
            $localeActions = is_array($permissions['actions'] ?? null) ? $permissions['actions'] : [];

            foreach (array_keys($modules) as $module) {
                if (! array_key_exists($module, $localeModules)) {
                    $missing[] = sprintf('%s: permissions.modules.%s', $locale, $module);
                }
            }
            foreach (array_keys($actions) as $action) {
                if (! array_key_exists($action, $localeActions)) {
                    $missing[] = sprintf('%s: permissions.actions.%s', $locale, $action);
                }
            }
        }

        sort($missing, SORT_STRING);

        $this->assertSame(
            [],
            $missing,
            sprintf(
                "%d permission labels are missing. Run `php artisan permissions:export-label-skeleton` — it writes a "
                ."COMPLETE block per locale to storage/app/permission-labels/.\n%s",
                count($missing),
                implode("\n", $missing),
            ),
        );
    }

    /** The three bundles are the ONLY source; there is no per-permission label, by design. */
    public function test_the_three_locales_declare_the_same_label_keys(): void
    {
        $shapes = [];
        foreach (self::LOCALES as $locale) {
            $bundle = $this->bundle($locale);
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $permissions */
            $permissions = is_array($bundle['permissions'] ?? null) ? $bundle['permissions'] : [];
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $localeModules */
            $localeModules = is_array($permissions['modules'] ?? null) ? $permissions['modules'] : [];
            /** @var array<string, mixed> $localeActions */
            $localeActions = is_array($permissions['actions'] ?? null) ? $permissions['actions'] : [];

            $keys = [...array_keys($localeModules), ...array_keys($localeActions)];
            sort($keys, SORT_STRING);
            $shapes[$locale] = $keys;
        }

        $this->assertSame($shapes['en'], $shapes['fr'], 'fr declares a different set of label keys than en.');
        $this->assertSame($shapes['en'], $shapes['ar'], 'ar declares a different set of label keys than en.');
    }

    /** @return array<string, mixed> */
    private function bundle(string $locale): array
    {
        /** @var array<string, mixed> $decoded */
        $decoded = json_decode(
            (string) file_get_contents(base_path(sprintf('../web/src/locales/%s/common.json', $locale))),
            true,
            512,
            JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
        );

        return $decoded;
    }
}
~~~

## Task 1.18: `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` over a versioned YAML runbook — **exact order, not presence**

**Gate r9's minor m-3, applied; plan gate r1's B1-5 and M1-5, applied on top.** The invariant is correct and stays; **parsing prose is brittle** — wording or Markdown layout can break the build without changing deployment, while semantically equivalent prose can evade a simplistic parser. The authoritative sequence therefore lives in a small **versioned machine-readable runbook**, and both the test and the human-facing documentation consume or validate **that** source.

**Three rev-2 corrections:**

1. **The YAML now carries the dry run**, as step 2 of every catalogue-changing wave from 1 onward, **after** `tenants:migrate-rolling`. Rev 1's YAML omitted it while rev 1's human Deploy table put it **first, before the migration** — and the first dry run would then have failed on missing `template_key` / `template_version` / `customised_at` / `is_system` columns (BLOCKER B1-5).
2. **The test asserts EXACT ORDER, not presence** (MAJOR M1-5). Rev 1's step 2 said only that each named command should "appear somewhere in the YAML", under which rev 1's own dry-run-before-migration row would have **passed**. The comparison is now `assertSame($yamlSteps, $documentSteps)` per wave — same commands, same order, same count.
3. **The human table is GENERATED from the YAML**, not written beside it. `php artisan permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=1` prints the Markdown rows, and the test asserts **this plan's** rows are **byte-identical** to that output after whitespace normalisation. A prose row that disagrees fails with the wave name, the expected row and the found row. **The spec's table is produced by the same renderer but is not yet asserted** — the test holds one `PLAN` constant (plan gate r3, minor 2) — and extending it to a list of documents is **R-w1-17**, wave 2a.

**Three rev-3 corrections on top:**

4. **Every step is a `{command, note}` MAPPING, not a bare scalar** (plan gate r2, MAJOR **M2-4**). Rev 2's YAML held plain scalars and no `note:` field, while its renderer took the Notes column *"from an optional `note:` key per step"* and the rendered table it was supposed to equal carried five substantive notes. **Those outputs cannot be byte-equal**, so rule (d) could never have passed. The YAML now carries the note beside the command, which is the option that keeps the Notes column *and* keeps generation honest; the alternative — dropping Notes from the compared output — was rejected because the notes are the half of that table an operator actually reads.
5. **Rules (b) and (c) read `step.command`**, not the step. That is the only change to the rule set, and it is stated so a reviewer checks the right field.
6. **`RenderPermissionDeployTable` and `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` are supplied as bodies** (plan gate r2, **B2-5**): rev 2 gave YAML and a four-line `assertSame` fragment.

`apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml`:

~~~yaml
# THE AUTHORITY for every wave's permission deploy sequence. The wave rows in
# docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md §8, in
# this wave's plan, and in the deploy runbook are VALIDATED AGAINST this file and
# GENERATED from it; this file is not generated from them.
#
# Every step is a {command, note} MAPPING. A bare scalar cannot produce the
# Notes column the human tables carry, which is what made rev 2's rule (d)
# unsatisfiable (plan gate r2, M2-4).
#
# Rules PermissionDeploySequenceTest enforces, all over `step.command`:
#   (a) no fleet-scoped deploy step invokes `permissions:sync` — the per-tenant
#       command may appear ONLY under `remediation:`, with an explicit --tenants=
#       or --tenant= argument, never as a wave's deploy step;
#   (b) no deploy step invokes `tenants:run permissions:sync`,
#       `tenants:run permissions:ensure` or `tenants:seed` in ANY option shape;
#   (c) every wave from 1 onward whose catalogue_changes is true lists
#       `permissions:sync-fleet` AFTER `tenants:migrate-rolling` and BEFORE
#       `permission:cache-reset`, and lists the `--dry-run` invocation AFTER
#       `tenants:migrate-rolling` too — sync reads and writes the roles template
#       columns, so a dry run before the migration fails on missing columns;
#   (d) THIS WAVE'S plan Deploy table is BYTE-EQUAL to
#       `permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=1` after whitespace
#       normalisation — EXACT ORDER, not command presence, Notes column
#       included. Scope note (plan gate r3, minor 2): the test holds ONE
#       document constant and reads only the wave-1 plan, so rule (d) is
#       stated for that document alone. The spec's and the other wave plans'
#       tables are generated from this same file by the same renderer, but
#       nothing yet ASSERTS them; widening the constant to a list is a
#       wave-2a follow-up, recorded as R-w1-17. Claiming coverage the test
#       does not have is worse than a narrow rule.
version: 3
waves:
  - wave: "0b"
    catalogue_changes: true
    # ensure-fleet is allow-listed for 0b ONLY; wave 1 deletes it.
    steps:
      - command: "bash scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh"
        note: "the two-invocation wrapper, and the only writer of the ensure status file"
      - command: "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
        note: "the boot-time global flush stays as a belt"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
        note: "the map's input is still the seeder in 0b"
    remediation:
      - command: "php artisan permissions:ensure-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug> --keys=<keys> --grant-to=<role>"
        note: "0b only; wave 1 deletes the command"
  - wave: "1"
    catalogue_changes: true
    steps:
      - command: "php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling"
        note: "the four `roles` columns, the two partial uniques, the three CHECKs. **First, always** — sync's adoption and delta logic reads and writes `template_key`, `template_version`, `customised_at` and `is_system`, so a dry run before this fails on missing columns."
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run"
        note: "the only supported way to preview a deploy's authorization delta. WRITE-free and **not** failure-free: a rename collision, a not-ready tenant or a missing `admin` makes it exit non-zero, and a non-zero dry run means the applying run must not be started."
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet"
        note: "**never** `tenants:run permissions:sync`; **never** the per-tenant `permissions:sync` without `--tenant=`; **there is no `--force`**. All three prohibitions are enforced by `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` against the YAML runbook."
      - command: "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
        note: "the boot-time global flush stays as a belt"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
        note: "the map's input is now the registry"
    remediation:
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug>"
        note: "each selector must resolve to exactly one tenant"
  - wave: "2a"
    catalogue_changes: false
    steps:
      - command: "php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling"
        note: "**First, always** — the same reason as wave 1"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run"
        note: "WRITE-free and **not** failure-free"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet"
        note: "**there is no `--force`**"
      - command: "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
        note: "the boot-time global flush stays as a belt"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
        note: "the map's input is the registry"
    remediation:
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug>"
        note: "each selector must resolve to exactly one tenant"
  - wave: "2b"
    catalogue_changes: true
    steps:
      - command: "php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling"
        note: "**First, always** — the `users` migration ships here"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run"
        note: "WRITE-free and **not** failure-free"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet"
        note: "**there is no `--force`**"
      - command: "php artisan permission:cache-reset"
        note: "the boot-time global flush stays as a belt"
      - command: "php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map"
        note: "the map's input is the registry"
    remediation:
      - command: "php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=<id-or-slug>"
        note: "each selector must resolve to exactly one tenant"
~~~
