**`permissions:render-deploy-table`, in full:**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Console\Commands;

use Illuminate\Console\Command;
use Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml;

/**
 * Print the Markdown deploy table for one wave (or all of them) FROM the
 * versioned runbook. The human tables in the spec and in each wave plan are
 * replaced by this output and compared to it byte-for-byte by
 * PermissionDeploySequenceTest.
 *
 * Editing one of those tables by hand fails the build. That is the point: this
 * is the third round in which a plan's prose disagreed with its machine-readable
 * twin, so the guard, not the correction, is the deliverable.
 */
final class RenderPermissionDeployTable extends Command
{
    protected $signature = 'permissions:render-deploy-table
        {--wave= : render one wave, e.g. 1 or 2a; omit for every wave}';

    protected $description = 'Render the Markdown deploy table for a wave from the versioned permission deploy runbook.';

    public const RUNBOOK = 'tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml';

    public function handle(): int
    {
        /** @var array{version: int, waves: list<array{wave: string, catalogue_changes: bool, steps: list<array{command: string, note: string}>, remediation?: list<array{command: string, note: string}>}>} $runbook */
        $runbook = Yaml::parseFile(base_path(self::RUNBOOK));

        $waveOption = $this->option('wave');
        $wanted = is_string($waveOption) && $waveOption !== '' ? $waveOption : null;

        $rendered = 0;
        foreach ($runbook['waves'] as $wave) {
            if ($wanted !== null && $wave['wave'] !== $wanted) {
                continue;
            }
            foreach (self::render($wave['steps']) as $line) {
                $this->line($line);
            }
            $rendered++;
        }

        if ($rendered === 0) {
            $this->error(sprintf('no wave "%s" in %s.', (string) $wanted, self::RUNBOOK));

            return self::FAILURE;
        }

        return self::SUCCESS;
    }

    /**
     * ONE renderer, used by the command AND by the test — so the test cannot
     * pass against a formatting the command does not produce.
     *
     * @param  list<array{command: string, note: string}>  $steps
     * @return list<string>
     */
    public static function render(array $steps): array
    {
        $lines = ['| # | Step | Notes |', '|---:|---|---|'];
        foreach ($steps as $index => $step) {
            $lines[] = sprintf('| %d | `%s` | %s |', $index + 1, $step['command'], $step['note']);
        }

        return $lines;
    }
}
~~~

**`PermissionDeploySequenceTest`, in full** — the fourth of the tests plan gate r2 named as the only oracle for its property:

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace Tests\Architecture;

use App\Console\Commands\RenderPermissionDeployTable;
use PHPUnit\Framework\TestCase;
use Symfony\Component\Yaml\Yaml;

/**
 * The versioned runbook is the AUTHORITY; the prose is validated against it.
 *
 * Rule (d) compares ORDERED LISTS and the rendered table BYTE-FOR-BYTE, not
 * command presence — presence is what let rev 1's dry-run-before-migration row
 * pass a test that was supposed to catch exactly it.
 */
final class PermissionDeploySequenceTest extends TestCase
{
    private const RUNBOOK = __DIR__.'/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml';

    /** `permissions:ensure-fleet` is allow-listed for wave 0b ONLY — a constant naming the wave, never a regex. */
    private const ENSURE_FLEET_ALLOWED_IN_WAVE = '0b';

    /**
     * Rule (d) is asserted over THIS document only. One constant, one plan — the
     * spec's table and the other wave plans' tables come out of the same
     * renderer but are not asserted here, and the YAML comment says so rather
     * than implying coverage this test does not have (plan gate r3, minor 2).
     * Widening it to a list is R-w1-17, wave 2a.
     */
    private const PLAN = __DIR__.'/../../../../docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md';

    /** @return array{version: int, waves: list<array{wave: string, catalogue_changes: bool, steps: list<array{command: string, note: string}>, remediation?: list<array{command: string, note: string}>}>} */
    private function runbook(): array
    {
        /** @var array{version: int, waves: list<array{wave: string, catalogue_changes: bool, steps: list<array{command: string, note: string}>, remediation?: list<array{command: string, note: string}>}>} $parsed */
        $parsed = Yaml::parseFile(self::RUNBOOK);

        return $parsed;
    }

    /** Rule (a). The per-tenant command is a REMEDIATION tool, never a deploy step. */
    public function test_no_deploy_step_invokes_the_per_tenant_command(): void
    {
        foreach ($this->runbook()['waves'] as $wave) {
            foreach ($wave['steps'] as $index => $step) {
                self::assertDoesNotMatchRegularExpression(
                    '/\bpermissions:sync(?!-fleet)\b/',
                    $step['command'],
                    sprintf(
                        'wave %s step %d invokes the PER-TENANT permissions:sync as a deploy step. It may appear only '
                        .'under remediation:, with an explicit --tenant= or --tenants=.',
                        $wave['wave'],
                        $index + 1,
                    ),
                );
            }

            foreach ($wave['remediation'] ?? [] as $step) {
                if (preg_match('/\bpermissions:sync(?!-fleet)\b/', $step['command']) === 1) {
                    self::assertMatchesRegularExpression(
                        '/--tenants?=/',
                        $step['command'],
                        sprintf('wave %s remediation names permissions:sync without an explicit tenant selector.', $wave['wave']),
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /** Rule (b). The three shapes that discard child exit statuses or reseed destructively. */
    public function test_no_deploy_step_reseeds_or_runs_per_tenant_children(): void
    {
        foreach ($this->runbook()['waves'] as $wave) {
            foreach ($wave['steps'] as $index => $step) {
                foreach (['tenants:run', 'tenants:seed'] as $forbidden) {
                    self::assertStringNotContainsString(
                        $forbidden,
                        $step['command'],
                        sprintf('wave %s step %d invokes `%s`, which discards child exit statuses or reseeds destructively (I-20).', $wave['wave'], $index + 1, $forbidden),
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /** Rule (c). EXACT ORDER: migrate, then dry run, then apply, then cache reset. */
    public function test_every_catalogue_changing_wave_orders_migrate_dry_run_apply_cache(): void
    {
        foreach ($this->runbook()['waves'] as $wave) {
            if ($wave['wave'] === '0b' || $wave['catalogue_changes'] !== true) {
                continue;
            }

            $commands = array_map(static fn (array $step): string => $step['command'], $wave['steps']);
            $migrate = $this->indexOf($commands, 'tenants:migrate-rolling');
            $dryRun = $this->indexOf($commands, 'permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run');
            $apply = $this->indexOfExact($commands, 'php artisan permissions:sync-fleet');
            $cache = $this->indexOf($commands, 'permission:cache-reset');

            self::assertGreaterThanOrEqual(0, $migrate, "wave {$wave['wave']} has no tenants:migrate-rolling step.");
            self::assertGreaterThan($migrate, $dryRun, "wave {$wave['wave']}: the dry run must follow tenants:migrate-rolling — sync reads and writes the roles template columns, so a dry run before the migration fails on missing columns.");
            self::assertGreaterThan($dryRun, $apply, "wave {$wave['wave']}: the applying sync must follow its own dry run.");
            self::assertGreaterThan($apply, $cache, "wave {$wave['wave']}: permission:cache-reset must follow the applying sync.");
        }
    }

    /** `permissions:ensure-fleet` survives in wave 0b and nowhere else. */
    public function test_ensure_fleet_is_allow_listed_for_wave_0b_only(): void
    {
        foreach ($this->runbook()['waves'] as $wave) {
            if ($wave['wave'] === self::ENSURE_FLEET_ALLOWED_IN_WAVE) {
                continue;
            }
            foreach ([...$wave['steps'], ...($wave['remediation'] ?? [])] as $step) {
                self::assertStringNotContainsString(
                    'permissions:ensure',
                    $step['command'],
                    sprintf('wave %s names an `ensure` command; wave 1 deletes them.', $wave['wave']),
                );
            }
        }
    }

    /**
     * Rule (d), for THIS plan. BYTE-EQUAL after whitespace normalisation,
     * through the SAME renderer the command uses. The method name says `plans`
     * singular on purpose: self::PLAN is one document (plan gate r3, minor 2).
     */
    public function test_the_plans_deploy_table_is_byte_equal_to_the_rendered_output(): void
    {
        $wave = null;
        foreach ($this->runbook()['waves'] as $candidate) {
            if ($candidate['wave'] === '1') {
                $wave = $candidate;
                break;
            }
        }
        self::assertNotNull($wave, 'the runbook has no wave 1.');

        $expected = array_map($this->normalise(...), RenderPermissionDeployTable::render($wave['steps']));
        $found = array_map($this->normalise(...), $this->tableFromMarkdown(self::PLAN));

        self::assertSame(
            $expected,
            $found,
            "the plan's Deploy table is not byte-equal to `php artisan permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=1`. "
            .'That table is GENERATED; edit the runbook and re-render, never the table.',
        );
    }

    /**
     * The first `| # | Step | Notes |` table in the document, read to its first
     * non-table line. Anchoring on the header row rather than on a section title
     * keeps the test independent of the prose around it.
     *
     * @return list<string>
     */
    private function tableFromMarkdown(string $path): array
    {
        $lines = explode("\n", (string) file_get_contents($path));
        $rows = [];
        $inTable = false;

        foreach ($lines as $line) {
            if (! $inTable && $this->normalise($line) === '| # | Step | Notes |') {
                $inTable = true;
                $rows[] = $line;

                continue;
            }
            if ($inTable) {
                if (! str_starts_with(trim($line), '|')) {
                    break;
                }
                $rows[] = $line;
            }
        }

        return $rows;
    }

    /** Collapse runs of whitespace and trim: layout is not the contract, content and order are. */
    private function normalise(string $line): string
    {
        return trim((string) preg_replace('/\s+/', ' ', $line));
    }

    /** @param list<string> $commands */
    private function indexOf(array $commands, string $needle): int
    {
        foreach ($commands as $index => $command) {
            if (str_contains($command, $needle)) {
                return $index;
            }
        }

        return -1;
    }

    /** @param list<string> $commands */
    private function indexOfExact(array $commands, string $needle): int
    {
        foreach ($commands as $index => $command) {
            if ($command === $needle) {
                return $index;
            }
        }

        return -1;
    }
}
~~~

> **The 0b row changed shape in rev 2, and that is not cosmetic.** Wave 0b rev 2's BLOCKER B0b-6 replaced its two bare `permissions:ensure-fleet` invocations with **one shell wrapper**, `scripts/permissions-ensure-0b.sh`, which is the only writer of the ensure status file. The runbook records the wrapper, so rule (b) is checkable and rev 1's trailing space after the ellipsis (`--keys=… `) — which would have broken byte-exact comparison — is gone with the row that carried it.

> **`version:` is `3` in rev 3**, because the step shape changed from scalar to `{command, note}`. A consumer that reads `version: 2`'s scalars against this file gets a type error rather than a silently wrong table, which is what a version field is for.

> **Why this guard matters more than the one-word correction that produced it.** Rev 8 of the spec's wave-2 row said `permissions:sync` — a command whose signature takes **one** `--tenant` or otherwise operates on the already-current tenant context, i.e. **no** tenant context at all in an entrypoint or a deploy shell. Wave 2 adds six manifest permissions **and** a fleet-wide `users` migration, so one context-free `permissions:sync` would have synchronised **zero** tenants — or exactly one, if a stray context existed — while the deploy reported success and every other tenant's `admin` silently lacked the new keys. **This is the second round in which a deploy row named the wrong entry point, and the third in which the plan's own prose disagreed with its machine-readable twin**, which is why the guard, not the correction, is the deliverable.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1** — write the YAML and `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` enforcing rules (a)–(d) over it. `permissions:ensure-fleet` is allow-listed **for wave 0b only**, and the allow-list is a constant naming the wave, not a regex.
- [ ] **Step 2 — `permissions:render-deploy-table`** (body above). Its `render()` is `public static` **on purpose**: the test calls the same method, so the test cannot pass against a formatting the command does not produce. The plan's and the spec's Deploy tables are **replaced by its output**.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the exact-order and byte-equality assertions** (body above): `test_every_catalogue_changing_wave_orders_migrate_dry_run_apply_cache` compares **positions**, and `test_the_plans_deploy_table_is_byte_equal_to_the_rendered_output` compares the **rendered lines**, Notes column included, after collapsing whitespace runs. Presence-only comparison is what let rev 1's dry-run-before-migration row pass; ordered-index and byte-equal comparison is the fix.
      > **Whitespace normalisation is `preg_replace('/\s+/', ' ', …)` plus `trim()` and nothing else.** It exists so a Markdown formatter that pads a column does not fail the build; it must **not** normalise punctuation, backticks or emphasis, or the guard stops catching a re-worded note — which is one of the three things rule (d) exists to catch.
- [ ] **Step 4 — three reds, each pasted into the handback:**
      1. add `php artisan permissions:sync` to wave 2a's YAML `steps` — the test must fail naming the wave and the command (rule a);
      2. **swap** wave 1's first two steps so the dry run precedes the migration — the test must fail naming wave 1 and the ordering rule (rule c). *This is the specific red that rev 1 could not produce.*
      3. edit one word of the plan's rendered Deploy table — the test must fail naming the wave, the expected row and the found row (rule d);
      4. **delete a step's `note:` from the YAML** — `RenderPermissionDeployTable::render()` then throws on the missing key and rule (d) fails. *This is the specific red rev 2 could not produce, because its YAML had no notes to delete and its rendered table could never have equalled the plan's* (plan gate r2, **M2-4**).
      Restore after each.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run, format, stage, commit:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w1/apps/api
      php artisan test tests/Architecture/PermissionDeploySequenceTest.php
      ./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app/Console/Commands/RenderPermissionDeployTable.php
      ./vendor/bin/pint --test app/Console/Commands/RenderPermissionDeployTable.php tests/Architecture/PermissionDeploySequenceTest.php
      git add apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/PermissionDeploySequenceTest.php \
              apps/api/app/Console/Commands/RenderPermissionDeployTable.php \
              docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md
      ```
      Subject: **`Phase 1.18: add PermissionDeploySequenceTest over a versioned YAML runbook, with exact-order validation of the human tables`**, both trailers.

## Reviewer gate

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on the whole diff.** Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w1` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9.1
> §§4.2, 4.3.0a, 4.3.1–4.3.5, 4.5, 4.6.5, 4.7, 4.9, 5.2, 6 and 8's wave-1 row, and
> against this plan. Cite `file:line` for every claim; verify against the tree,
> never from the diff alone.
>
> 1. **Does the registry carry exactly the old catalogue, and is it 327?** Independently
>    derive the key set from the deleted seeder array (read it from `dev`), add the
>    lane's two wrapper keys, wave 0b's nineteen and **T2's two transfer keys**, apply
>    the rename map, and compare to `PermissionRegistry::keys()`. Name every key that
>    moved, was added or was lost, and say which is intentional. **If your count is 323
>    you read the wrong method: `dev`'s `permissionNames()` is the lane's
>    `legacyPermissionNames()`. If it is 325, `lane/t2-receipt-spine` is not an ancestor
>    of the base you read — that is an entry-condition failure, not a catalogue defect.**
> 2. **Was every commit green?** Walk `Phase 1.4a` … `Phase 1.4i` and confirm the
>    unassigned fixture shrinks monotonically to zero and that no commit ships a test
>    whose oracle arrives later.
> 3. **Is adoption really before every grant write?** Read `sync()` top to bottom
>    and list the steps in order. Then construct the tenant state under which a
>    customised role would be adopted pristine, and show why it cannot occur.
> 4. **Is the exact diff safe, and is the rerun genuinely zero-write?** For each of
>    the three role kinds, name the writes step 6 performs and prove a **custom** role
>    can lose nothing. Then find the `template_version` write and confirm it is
>    conditional, and find the query-log assertion that proves the second run writes
>    no row. A test asserting "no exception" is not that claim.
> 5. **Is `provisioning_source` written in exactly one case?** Find every write to
>    that column in the diff. There must be one, on a `general_manager` sync
>    creates in the same statement, on a tenant with no marker. Verify the lane's
>    CHECK is satisfied by construction on that path.
> 6. **Are there exactly two triggers?** Grep for `DatabaseMigrated`, for every
>    caller of `PermissionSyncService::sync()`, and for anything in a listener or
>    provider that could reach it. Then read the entrypoint and say what a deploy
>    does with the flag `false`.
> 7. **Did the stopgap go in ONE commit?** Confirm all eight artefacts, the three
>    superseded tests and the one rename are in the same commit as
>    `PermissionSyncService`, and that `SyncRollback` lands there too — there
>    must be no commit in which the dry-run path has no sentinel.
> 8. **Does the lock still make one domain?** Confirm wave 1 did not redefine the
>    key, the hash function or the row order; that the three new writers are in
>    `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`; that `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo` is in
>    **`LOCK_INHERITED_FROM`** with both entry points named, not in `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`;
>    and that `INITIALIZATION_ONLY` did not grow. Then check `RoleController::update`
>    writes `customised_at` in the **same** transaction as its `syncPermissions()`.
> 9. **Is the marker's counter split honest?** Name the ten mutations and the two
>    gauges, and confirm no gauge is asserted zero on a re-run anywhere.
> 10. **Dry run:** prove a `BLOCKED` dry run exits non-zero, on the per-tenant
>     command and on the fleet, and that a missing `admin` exits 1 on both paths.
>     Then read `plan()` and `execute()` and answer three things mechanically:
>     is there **any** write outside `execute()`; does **every** path on which
>     `outcome->isGreen()` is false leave the transaction by `throw`; and does
>     the dry run compute its step-6 deltas from the **virtual** role state
>     rather than re-querying `roles`? A `return` on a non-green apply is the
>     defect B2-1 named and it commits steps 1-7.
> 10a. **Tenancy:** confirm `permissions:sync --tenant=` calls
>     `tenancy()->initialize()` before the service and `tenancy()->end()` in a
>     `finally` **guarded on having started it**, and that
>     `SyncPermissionsTenantSelectorTest` uses two physical tenant databases
>     rather than two team ids on one.
> 10b. **Provisioning:** confirm a non-green sync **throws**
>     `TenantPermissionSyncFailed`, that the marker is emitted before the throw,
>     and that a test proves `assignDefaultRoles()` did not run.
> 11. **Compatibility mode:** confirm steps 1–4 run, step 5 does **not**, and the
>     `--tenant` refusal is exit 2.
> 12. **The static guards:** confirm the PHPStan baseline absorbs only pre-existing
>     sites, that the rule reads its key list from a generated file rather than
>     booting the app, and that `RoutePermissionLiteralTest` **accepts** the
>     `'can:'.Enum::Case->value` form — a guard that rejects the migration target
>     is a wall. Then check the ordering: `permission-registry-keys.json` must be
>     in **`Phase 1.16`'s** commit, not `Phase 1.17`'s, or the history is red
>     between them. And confirm the wave's own new route is one of the test's
>     accepted-shape cases rather than a baselined literal.
> 13. **Deploy order:** confirm the YAML, this plan's Deploy table and the spec's
>     wave-1 row all put `tenants:migrate-rolling` **before** every sync invocation
>     including the dry run, and that `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` compares
>     **ordered lists**, not command presence.
> 14. **SoD:** re-derive the baseline from the registry and compare to the
>     eighteen-row table. Confirm `purchase-orders.approve` is **not** declared
>     anywhere. Say whether O-5 was answered and, if not, whether the ceiling is
>     the right one to ship.
> 15. **Audit:** confirm the `AuditEvent` guard change does not alter the existing
>     company path, that `$this->companyId = $companyId ?? ''` is present (the
>     property is non-nullable), that `RoleSyncedV1` is registered in the subscriber,
>     and that a row actually lands.
> 16. **Overlap and the seeder:** confirm no other lane edits
>     `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, that the seven references are each disposed of
>     by an executed command rather than by inspection, and that
>     `RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest` is green and unedited.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory**, for the generated map and the label bundles. Ask specifically: is `permissionsMap.generated.ts` byte-identical across two generations **and** against the committed artifact (W-LOT's determinism gate)? Is `PERMISSION_MODULES` typed `as const` and consumed nowhere as a decision input (it is grouping data, not authorization)? Are all three locales complete, and is any Arabic or French value an English placeholder — and if so, is it recorded as a residual rather than shipped as done? Did the wave touch `usePermissions.ts` or `uiAliasPermissions.ts`? It must **not** — those are wave 2a.

**`fiscal-pos-reviewer` — advisory**, for the fourteen `pos.*` / `pos_orders.*` keys entering the deprecation set. Deprecation does not change runtime behaviour (the row survives one release), but the POS reads its approval scopes from `pos.approve_*` and a mis-deprecated key would remove a manager override. Ask: is every deprecated `pos.*` key genuinely unreachable from `apps/pos/src`, and does `permissions:report-dead`'s permission-shaped rule correctly exclude the `t('reports.view')` i18n hit?

No inventory, treasury or imports reviewer: wave 1 declares those modules' permissions but changes no inventory, treasury or import behaviour. **If a manifest's `templateDefaults` differs from the post-0b grant map for any Inventory or Treasury key, that is a behaviour change and the owning reviewer becomes mandatory** — say so in the handback rather than deciding it silently.

## Handback

`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w1-<ISO date>.md` — contents enumerated in Task 1.19 step 10.

### Residuals to record in it

| # | Residual | Owner / wave |
|---|---|---|
| **R-w1-1** | The eight **role-name** authorization sites (`CreateDraftCountingRequest.php:35`, `InventoryCountingController.php:782,866,905,975,1297,1494`, `DiscountPermissionResolver.php:39`) still bypass token narrowing. Wave 1 ships `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` and its shrink-only baseline so no ninth can be added; it converts none. Conversion is **wave 2a** and is a hard **entry condition of wave 2b** — `service-accounts.issue-token` stays unrouted until `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` is green, because issuing a narrowing token while a role-name idiom can bypass the narrowing would ship the intersection guarantee as a claim rather than a property. | wave 2a / 2b |
| **R-w1-2** | Any French or Arabic label shipped as an English placeholder. `permissions:export-label-skeleton` produces the complete file; the translation is human work with a named owner. | translator |
| **R-w1-3** | The **PHPStan literal baseline is large**. The door is closed behind it (a new literal is an error on day one) and the list only shrinks. The exact counts are **measured in the lane**, not carried from a prior revision. **Wave 3 empties it.** | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-4** | `LotActionPermissionDelta` survives as **dead code**. Sync never called it, so retirement is a deletion with no re-pointing and no behavioural change — once every tenant carries the marker. Likewise `SystemRoleName` and `SystemRoleTemplate` remain two enums with one asserted-equal value. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-5** | The `settings` **bare-manage/CRUD grandfather** is the only entry in `MANAGE_CRUD_GRANDFATHERED`. Retiring it means deciding whether `settings.manage` subsumes `settings.update` or the two gate genuinely different surfaces — an **owner question**, not a wave-1 rename. The list is shrink-only. **The three *qualified*-manage resources (`users`, `vehicles`, `catalog-cart`) are NOT exceptions**: a qualified `manage` is a narrowing of one facet and the rule is stated on the bare verb. | owner / wave 3 |
| **R-w1-6** | `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` does not exist. Step 3 **reports** orphans and never deletes; the `orphans` gauge will be non-zero on any tenant carrying a leftover row, forever, and that is not a failure. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-7** | **`marketplace.admin`** gates seller administration inside the tenant API yet is a platform-scoped capability — and under `admin = activeKeys()` **every tenant admin now holds it**. It is unreachable today (the module sits behind a kill-switch defaulting to false), which is why the correct fix — re-gate those routes on `super_admin` and deprecate the key — is wave 3 + ticket `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-marketplace-admin-platform-scope.md`. Inventing a `platformScoped` flag for one dormant key would be worse than the problem. **Verify the kill-switch default is still `false` at merge**, and say so in the handback. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-8** | **O-5 / OQ-3**, if unanswered at merge: the SoD baseline ships at **eighteen** and the ceiling with it. The recommended retirement is three from `manager` (+ the mirror on `general_manager`). The ratchet guarantees the number can only fall, so a later ruling is a follow-up lane, not a rework. | **owner** |
| **R-w1-9** | **O-6 / OQ-4**: whether `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT` becomes production-default `true` after the soak. This wave ships it `false` and produces the soak evidence the decision needs. The conservative alternative costs one runbook line and is a real option; it is worse only because a step a human can forget is a step a human will forget. | **owner**, at the end of the soak |
| **R-w1-10** | The **5-minute `/auth/me` staleness window** (EC-17) is untouched: on cold load Zustand hydrates roles/permissions from `localStorage` before `/auth/me` resolves, and `AuthProvider`'s `staleTime` means an open tab can render a five-minute-old permission set. It is a **UX-only** exposure — every mutating request is still authorized server-side — and it is recorded, not silently accepted. | out of scope |
| **R-w1-11** | Step 7 (**deprecation replacement grants**) is a **provable no-op in this wave**: all 22 deprecations carry `replacedBy: null`. The branch is implemented and tested with a throw-away deprecation, but it has **never run against real data**. The first wave that deprecates a key *with* a successor should treat its first fleet run as the branch's real evidence. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-12** | `restore` and `override` are declared `PermissionVerb` cases with **zero** keys using them. `PermissionVerbTest` snapshots that fact so it stays visible; the first key that uses either deletes one line of the test. | — |
| **R-w1-13** | **~30 test cases are named with their assertions and fixtures rather than pasted as bodies.** The eight classes and four oracle tests plan gate r2 named are supplied in full; the remainder are assertion-fill over fixtures this plan specifies. Enumerated in *Findings NOT applied*. | executing agent |
| **R-w1-14** | **`SyncPlan`'s six work lists are the sync's second representation of its own steps.** They cannot drift from `plan()` (one method builds them) and `execute()` asserts the applier agrees, but a wave that adds a tenth step must remember to add it in **both** halves. `PermissionSyncServiceTest` has no test for "a step exists in plan() but not in execute()", and a static one is not obviously worth its cost — recorded so the next wave decides deliberately. | wave 2a |
| **R-w1-15** | **`ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` duplicates the eight `SystemRoleTemplate` values as a constant**, because a PHPStan rule runs in its own process and must not boot Laravel to read an enum. `SystemRoleTemplateAlignmentTest` asserts the two lists are equal, so the duplication cannot drift silently — but it is a duplication, and wave 3 (which reconciles `SystemRoleName` and `SystemRoleTemplate`) should collapse it. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-17** | **`PermissionDeploySequenceTest` rule (d) covers ONE document.** The test holds a single `PLAN` constant and asserts byte-equality only for this wave's plan; the spec's §8 table and the other wave plans' tables are produced by the same `RenderPermissionDeployTable::render()` but nothing asserts them, so a hand-edit there is not caught. Widening the constant to a list of `(document, wave)` pairs is cheap and deliberate — it is deferred rather than claimed, because rev 3's prose claimed the coverage while the test did not have it (plan gate r3, minor 2). | wave 2a |
| **R-w1-18** | **`permissions:sync --compat` has no fleet, by design, and therefore no automated multi-installation evidence.** Compatibility mode is a **non-production topology** kept alive for legacy single-schema installations; EC-21..EC-21d exercise the command against a two-tenant shared schema in the PG lane, but no staging environment runs in this mode, so the branch never sees a real deploy. The first installation that needs it should treat its run as the branch's real evidence — the same posture R-w1-11 takes for deprecation replacements. | wave 3 |
| **R-w1-16** | **The entrypoint's dry-run gate doubles the boot-time fleet work** when `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT=true`: every tenant is planned twice. On the soak's fleet that is seconds; on a fleet an order of magnitude larger it is a number to measure before the production default flips. **Record the measured boot-time delta in the soak evidence** — it is an input to O-6/OQ-4 that rev 2 did not have. | **owner**, at the end of the soak |

## Deploy

**Generated from `apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/permission-deploy-runbook.yaml` (wave `1`) by `php artisan permissions:render-deploy-table --wave=1`, and validated against it byte-for-byte by `PermissionDeploySequenceTest`.** Editing this table by hand fails the build.

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | `php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling` | the four `roles` columns, the two partial uniques, the three CHECKs. **First, always** — sync's adoption and delta logic reads and writes `template_key`, `template_version`, `customised_at` and `is_system`, so a dry run before this fails on missing columns. |
| 2 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` | the only supported way to preview a deploy's authorization delta. WRITE-free and **not** failure-free: a rename collision, a not-ready tenant or a missing `admin` makes it exit non-zero, and a non-zero dry run means the applying run must not be started. |
| 3 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet` | **never** `tenants:run permissions:sync`; **never** the per-tenant `permissions:sync` without `--tenant=`; **there is no `--force`**. All three prohibitions are enforced by `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` against the YAML runbook. |
| 4 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | the boot-time global flush stays as a belt |
| 5 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | the map's input is now the registry |

**Deploy-checklist row.** *After deploy, confirm `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` reports `permissions_sync.healthy = true` — the **authenticated** super-admin endpoint at `apps/api/routes/api.php:83`; the public `/v1/health` is unchanged and still reports only `{status, timestamp}`. If it reports false, grep the Dokploy container log for the `PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET` aggregate line, which names every blocked or failed tenant id with its reason, and re-run `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=…` for those tenants — **each selector resolving to exactly one tenant**, per the step-1a contract — before considering the deploy complete.*

**Staging soak: one week with `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT=true`**, comparing each tenant's role-permission snapshot before and after every deploy and requiring **zero** unintended diffs. This is the direct replacement for the behaviour that caused I-20, so it gets the evidence bar that lane established, and it is the input to **O-6/OQ-4**.

**How a staging PUSH observes steps 1–3, given that nobody types them** (plan gate r2, MAJOR **M2-5**). The table above is the operator's sequence; with the soak flag on, the **entrypoint** is the operator, and it runs the same three steps in the same order:

| Deploy step | Who runs it on a staging push | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 `tenants:migrate-rolling` | the entrypoint, **unconditionally**, before the flagged block | `dev:apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:150-154` |
| 2 `permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` | the entrypoint, **first statement inside** the flagged block | Task 1.11's block |
| 3 `permissions:sync-fleet` | the entrypoint, **only inside the dry run's success branch** | Task 1.11's block |

So there is no window in which an image push applies before it previews, and a non-zero preview writes `permissions_sync=blocked`, **skips the apply**, and flips the admin health endpoint — which is how the operator learns, since they could not have interposed a command anyway. `EntrypointPermissionSyncShapeTest` asserts the three positions by line number, so this table is a property of the shell file rather than of this paragraph.

**With the flag OFF** — the production default this wave ships — steps 2 and 3 are the operator's, typed in the order above, and the entrypoint runs step 1 only.

---

## Rollback

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 1.19 (parity teardown) | the transitional test returns | — |
| Task 1.18 (deploy sequence) | the runbook guard disappears and the Deploy table stops being generated | — |
| Task 1.17 (FE map + labels) | the map's input returns to the seeder statics | Task 1.14 first (the statics must still be the array, not the shim). **No longer Task 1.16**: `permission-registry-keys.json` is generated and committed in **Task 1.16's own commit** (plan gate r2, B2-4), so reverting 1.17 leaves the rule's input in place and the forward history has no red window between the two |
| Task 1.16 (static guards) | the two rules, their baselines **and the registry key fixture** disappear together | — |
| Task 1.15 (`permissions:scaffold`) | the generator disappears | — |
| Task 1.14 (the shim) | **the seeder shim reverts to the array in the same revert commit** — a shim without a registry is a class calling a service that does not exist | Tasks 1.4 and 1.3 in the same revert |
| Task 1.13 (SoD) | the baseline and the rule disappear | — |
| Task 1.12 (`customised_at` writer) | the operator edit stops marking customisation | **Task 1.10 first** — sync would otherwise keep applying deltas to roles an operator has edited, which is I-20's shape |
| Task 1.11 (triggers) | the deploy trigger and the health check disappear | the entrypoint's **previous** block must be restored **in the same revert commit**, or a deploy runs no catalogue step at all |
| **Task 1.10 (sync + commands + the stopgap deletion)** | no sync exists **and no `permissions:ensure` exists either** — reverting this commit restores all eight 0b artefacts along with the deletion, because they are one commit. That is the point of the atomicity: there is no intermediate state in which neither writer exists | Tasks 1.12 and 1.11 first |
| Task 1.9 (`RoleSyncedV1`) | sync's writes stop being audited | Task 1.10's steps 6 and 7 emit it — revert Task 1.10 or accept an unaudited replacement grant |
| Task 1.8 (applier + marker repo) | — | Task 1.10 first |
| **Task 1.7 (the `roles` migration)** | **`down()` REFUSES while any row has `template_key IS NOT NULL`** — so a rollback after a sync is a deliberate act that first requires clearing adoption, and never silently discards which roles were system roles | clear adoption first, deliberately |
| Task 1.6 (report-dead) | the scanner disappears; the `deprecate()` wraps stay (they live in Task 1.4's manifests) | — |
| Tasks 1.4–1.5 (manifests, enums) | the registry empties | Task 1.14 in the same revert |
| Tasks 1.1–1.3 (verb, definition, registry) | the catalogue machinery disappears | everything above first |

**`permissions:sync` is additive**, so a rollback leaves extra `permissions` rows on every tenant. They are **inert** once no route and no template references them, and `permissions:prune-orphans` is **not** run as part of a rollback — it does not exist until wave 3, and it is a deliberate operator action even then.

**The one thing a revert cannot undo** is a `template_key` already written by a sync that ran on a live tenant. That is what the migration's refusing `down()` is for: it makes clearing adoption an explicit, auditable act rather than a side effect of a `git revert`.

---

| # | Finding | Where it is applied |
|---|---|---|
| **N4-1** | The File Structure row for `phpstan.neon` still said `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` goes under **`rules:`**, while Task 1.16 Step 1, the `neon` block and rev 3's own changelog minor all said **`services:`** — three places agreeing and one disagreeing. B3-4 makes it decisive: the role-name rule now takes **two** constructor arguments, and `rules:` accepts none. | The row is rewritten to say both rules go under `services:`, with each rule's argument count, and records that rev 4 corrected it |
| **N4-2** | Task 1.10 Step 7's PG run list omitted `SyncPermissionsTenantSelectorTest.php`, which Step 8 nonetheless **stages** — so the commit's own verification never ran the file it commits, and B3-3's oracle would have had no run of its own. | The file is added to Step 7's invocation, beside `PermissionWriteLockConcurrentWriterTest.php` |
| **N4-3** | A **second** `'Permission.php'` broad suffix exists in the plan, in `ReportDeadPermissions::DECLARATION_SUFFIXES` (Task 1.6). M3-2 names only the PHPStan literal guard, and a gate re-reading `Permission.php` after rev 4 would find this one and have to re-derive whether it was missed. | **Deliberately NOT narrowed, and the reason is written beside the constant.** The two lists fail in opposite directions: over-exempting in the literal guard silently opens a security gate; over-excluding here only removes a file from the *consumer* haystack, so a key can at worst be over-reported as **dead** — a false positive a human reads. The two real-tree files that match it broadly carry no dotted key and are not consumers either way. Recorded rather than changed, because narrowing it is scope the gate did not ask for |

### Findings NOT applied

**None.** All six blockers, both majors and all four minors were verified against code and applied. The register's *"Rejected false positives"* section was checked and none of its items was re-litigated: the 327 catalogue, the role totals, the six invalid-resource `legacy()` calls, `payments.reverse`'s SoD membership, the migration's enum-free `ALTER`s, `SyncRollback`'s own semantics, the fail-closed provisioning, the entrypoint's `set -e` nesting and the class-before-test document ordering all stand as rev 3 shipped them.

**The one residual rev 3 declared survives rev 4, smaller.** Roughly thirty test cases in Tasks 1.10, 1.11, 1.12 and 1.19 remain **named with their exact assertions and fixtures rather than pasted as bodies** (**R-w1-13**). Rev 4 adds **eight** more named cases in that style — the six blocker oracles, the M3-1 split's two halves and M3-2's exemption negatives — each with (a) its arrangement, (b) its exact assertions, (c) the rev-3 behaviour it is red against, and (d) an instruction to paste the red into the handback. The register's *"must be corrected or supplied before dispatch"* list is satisfied at that level of specification for every entry; a gate that wants the bodies pasted should say so and the round is cheap.

### New findings raised while applying rev 3

| # | Finding | Where it is applied |
|---|---|---|
| **N3-1** | Keying the virtual state by **role name** is sound only because `template_key` and `name` are equal by construction on every role wave 1 touches — but nothing in the tree *enforces* that, and rev 2's step 6 and step 8 re-queried by `template_key` while step 4 matched by `name`, so the two could silently disagree. | `refuseTemplateKeyNameMismatch()` runs before the virtual state is built and blocks with `reason=template_key_name_mismatch:<name>`; `PermissionSyncServiceTest::a_template_key_that_disagrees_with_its_role_name_is_blocked` pins it. Introduced **by** the fix, so guarding it is part of the fix |
| **N3-2** | Rev 2 computed the **orphan gauge** off the live `permissions` table, which on the applying path was post-create and on the dry-run path was not — so the same tenant reported two different `orphans=` numbers depending on mode. | Step 3 reads the **post-step-2 virtual set**, which is equal to rev 2's applying-path number by construction (step 2 only ever adds registry keys). Stated at step 3 |
| **N3-3** | A planner and an executor can disagree, and a planner whose numbers can disagree with the writer's is worse than no planner. | `execute()` compares the applier's returned `TemplateDelta` to the planned one and throws `SyncRollback` with `reason=plan_divergence:<template>` on any difference; `a_planner_executor_divergence_rolls_back` is its test |
| **N3-4** | `admin_role_missing` becomes **unreachable at planning time** once step 5 plans an admin in the virtual state — so keeping only the planning refusal would have made the register's *"the test proving zero writes on a forced `admin_role_missing`"* unwritable without a test double. | The refusal gains a **second, reachable home**: `verifyAdmin()` after `execute()`, with `admin_not_current` beside it. The forcing in the test is a real PostgreSQL `AFTER INSERT` trigger, and the plan says why it must be `AFTER` rather than `BEFORE` (a `BEFORE` trigger returning `NULL` makes `insert … returning "id"` fail, and the run would report `exception:` instead of the branch under test) |
| **N3-5** | `SyncDryRunRollback`'s name would have been wrong for three of its four callers after B2-1. | Renamed **`SyncRollback`**, exactly as rev 2's own docblock instructed for this trigger. Every reference is updated, and the Task 1.10 Step 7 grep adds the old name so it cannot survive in code |
| **N3-6** | `PERMISSION_MODULES` is `module => keys` on the PHP side and `key => module` on the TypeScript side; rev 2 showed the TS shape and left the inversion unstated. | `render()` inverts `byModule()` explicitly, `ksort`s the result, and emits `PermissionModule` as a derived type. The frontend reviewer is asked to confirm it is consumed as grouping data and never as an authorization input |
| **N3-7** | Rev 2's `Phase 1.4i` subject said *"close the 325-key catalogue"* and its eight siblings had no stated subject at all, so eight of nine commit messages were an unexpanded template. | All nine subjects are written out with their key and legacy counts, which reconcile to 327 / 59 / 22 |

### Findings NOT applied, with the reason — and the ONE explicit residual of rev 3

| Gate ID | Why not |
|---|---|
| **B2-5**, the remaining named-not-pasted test bodies | **Ten bodies are supplied** (the eight the register named, plus the exporter's `render()` and the baseline generator), and **the four the register called *"the only oracles"* are supplied or fully specified**: cross-database isolation, concurrency/rollback, query-log zero-write and the migration-constraint tests. **The residual, stated exactly:** roughly **thirty** further test cases in Tasks 1.10, 1.11, 1.12 and 1.19 remain **named rather than pasted**. Every one of them now carries, in prose, **(a) its exact assertions list and (b) its fixture setup** — see EC-9c, `PermissionSyncMarkerTest`'s five negatives and one positive, EC-7, EC-21/21a/21b, EC-37a/37b, EC-38, EC-39, EC-40, EC-B2-1a/b/c, EC-B2-3, `PosApprovalPermissionSecondLocationTest`, the six `TemplateReapplyTest` cases, the six `ScaffoldPermissionCommandTest` cases and the six `PermissionReportDeadScopeTest` cases. **This is a deliberate stopping point, not an oversight**: they are assertion-fill over fixtures this document already specifies, whereas the ten supplied bodies required designing AST traversal, source rewriting, YAML rendering, a planner and a rollback protocol — which is exactly the distinction the register drew. **A gate that wants them pasted should say so and the round is cheap; a gate that reads "named" as "undesigned" is reading it wrong.** |
| **M1-6**, the "author every test before its implementation" half | Applied as far as the document can carry it — every task's Step 1 is now *"write the test first"* and every red is named and reproducible — but the supplied class bodies are still printed **before** the test bodies in several tasks, because interleaving them at method granularity makes the plan unreadable as a dispatch document without changing what an executing agent produces. Recorded as an execution-discipline note for the handback, exactly as wave 0b rev 2 recorded the same critique: **write each test file before the implementation it covers, and if a red can only be produced by removing a branch you just wrote, say so in the evidence.** |
